Seven Sundays since the middle of April when the round went and somebody needed a doctor at ten o'clock, and on two of the seven somebody's mother was at home and waiting and was seen, because a nurse of twenty-three knows. **What she did about it was one line in her own day book with no mark between the two halves, and she is not going to either office, because if she goes the clinic is a place somebody has asked about, and being asked about is the whole of what this district has been doing for six months.** In about four years the file in the other building will say there was no Sunday service, and there was, and she will be sixty-eight, and that is not a tragedy, it is a Friday morning. Then the three words: *what was that.* **He did not hear the counter, which has made that noise every day for nineteen years, and it was not there for about four seconds and it was there again, and it did not come back.** She did not ask him to find it and did not ask him to describe it and did not ask whether a face or a place was attached to it, because **a person who is asked to find a sound starts looking for it, and looking for it is a thing you do with your hands, and then you have got a routine, and a routine about a sound is the fourth thing.** At about ten past one, at the counter, a woman of sixty-one: **there is not going to be a fifth comparison**, the first working day of the month was Monday the first of June and she did not do it, the number on the outside of the envelope is unchanged and is not going to change, **and she has wanted to write something next to it four times and is not going to, and the fourth time she has stopped saying that out loud is the one she means.** Field Book: two printed lists both correct and a form that does not exist; a woman's sentence taken apart and written on one line; **a sound that came and went for four seconds and the doctor who did not ask him to listen for it, and it is the first time in six months a person with a pen has been in the room with one of these and has not asked, and he has not written down whether that was her deciding or her not noticing**; the envelope, five months, the second half of her rule; ten routes, nine crossings, the tally unopened; the page home the hundred and ninety-first day, the token the hundred and seventy-seventh day, and the standing position.

### Chapter 123 — A Column About A Boy

**REPAIRED AT CR-043 in four places. (a) The plate in the narration about Wednesday the third of June is fifty-eight days, not fifty-nine, and the Field Book entry for Thursday the fourth keeps fifty-nine; both are right and both are printed. (b) The man of forty-one's nine words are the seventh time, which ascends from the fifth in Ch 117 and the sixth on the first of June in Ch 121, and the chapter now separates the two figures he was conflating — four times a day through the gate is not four times a day with a job in front of him. (c) The gate is at a quarter past seven no more; the gang is at half past seven. (d) The man of thirty-one no longer occupies four rooms above a fitter's shop in Ch 124's counter scene; that is the works clerk's address.**

**The failure, the seventh time, the same one, and the district knew the shape of it before he had got the pencil out.** Wednesday the third of June between about seven and half past nine, a table in a front room. **ROUND — MONTHLY RETURN (COURIER)**, six columns copied off a return the boy of seventeen fills in on a Monday and a Thursday, all six correct and in the right order, and then a seventh written first in about four seconds: *why this round is carried by one man.* One line, about eighty words, every word true, **no boy's name in it and no woman's and no department's, and it is the best thing he has written in two months, and he read it back and did not feel the thing he felt in March and April, and he has felt nothing four times now and has not decided what that is.** At about ten past seven on Thursday the fourth, at a gate, a man of forty-one on no list at all, nine seconds: **you have written a seventh column and it is about a boy.** Then: *seven.* And the rest of it, and it is the character of the whole volume: **that is seven of them and I have watched six and I have never once seen you learn it, and nobody in this district has.** The finding, from a works clerk of forty-six in four rooms above a fitter's shop at about eleven on the Thursday, in about eleven seconds, and it is not about the seventh column: **a round carried by one man and a round carried by two hand back the same six lines, the return is not asking whether the round was enough, it is asking whether the work was done, and a shortage is not work undone; in about four years somebody in a depot reads twelve perfect months, sees no shortage anywhere in them, and is right, and there is no column in that authority that will let him say the other thing.** And: **what is on the return about a boy of seventeen is his name once a week and a route through a gate, and a sentence in your handwriting on a form about a round is a document about a person, and he is seventeen and he is not going to be asked about it and he does not know it is on paper, and if it ever came to him he would not be able to get out from under it by being forty.** The resolution, and it is the eighth of eight and the first one anybody else can walk into: **on the top of his own table in his own office, face up, nothing on the outside of it, nothing on the back of it, a heading in the seventh column and eighty words in it, not in a drawer, not in a rack, not under a brick, not in a book in a coat, and he has not told anybody and the man of forty-one did not ask and the not-asking took four seconds.** *A table is not custody and a table is not hidden, and a thing left in the open in a room other people walk into is the first of the seven that can be produced against me, and I have chosen it and nobody asked me to.* And the clause written before the rest of it: **I am going to draw another one in about four months and I am not going to be able to help it, and the reason this one is on a table is not that I have stopped, and I am not going to let anybody in this district, including me, turn a custody decision into a reason for not writing.** Field Book: the eight resolutions in order — a tin, a coat, a yard, a table in the rain, a counter, a book, a sentence with a why in it, a table in his own office; six of the eight are places and two are sentences; the diagnosis unchanged since the fifteenth of March; the round's own return and the four columns of the round sheet; twenty-two days unasked and Tern not found; the plate at fifty-nine days; the second tin at twenty-three on the twenty-second of May and twenty-four on the twenty-ninth; the woman of sixty-eight on nothing added zero; the page home the hundred and ninety-third day and **the token the hundred and seventy-ninth day, the same figure as the page home carried on the twenty-first of May, a different register, and he has put the two of them side by side on purpose so that there is one place in the book where they are together and nobody is to take them together.**

### Chapter 124 — What Day Is The Figure Of

**REPAIRED AT CR-043 in four places. (a) The plate attached to Wednesday the third of June is fifty-eight days, not fifty-nine. (b) The woman of sixty-one no longer describes Milo Rook as a man of thirty-one in four rooms above a fitter's shop; she gives him no address, because that address is the works clerk's. (c) The Field Book no longer says the Monday is the day the post came in — the post comes in on Friday and the Monday is the day the sheet goes out, and the chapter now says so in the woman's mouth as well as in the book's. (d) The wrong-shape count is printed: this is the eighth in seven months, and Ch 125 is the ninth and Ch 126 the tenth, so the sequence ascends on the page instead of jumping.**

**The changed method and the recovery, and both of them are somebody else's work, and neither of them is a counter and neither is a drawer.** Friday the fifth of June. The rack at ten past ten and **the second tin at twenty-five**. A woman of sixty-one reads four returns out loud, standing up, in order, to a man of thirty-one who had not asked, and the reason is nineteen words of hers: **because in about four years these four sheets are going to be produced against somebody who lives in one of the buildings on them, and that person is not going to have been in this room, and being asked about a figure is a different thing from having heard a figure read out in a room by a person who was there when it was wrong. One of those two you can answer. The other one you can only be asked.** She does them in the order the post came in and not in the order of the days, because the days are somebody else's order. And then the question he asked her, three words: **what day is the figure of.** *Saturday.* **Every week, the figures on these are the count of the Saturday, and they go out on the Monday, and there is no column anywhere on this piece of paper for the day the figure is of, and there has not been one since about 1971, and the figures are a week old every single time anybody in this district reads one of them, and nobody has ever asked me, and not one of you has ever asked when a figure is of, and that is not because you are all fools, it is because every figure comes on a sheet with a date on the sheet and the date on the sheet is the Monday.** He walked out at about half past eleven and stood on a front step and went down about two hundred and forty entries and **not one entry is wrong and every one of them is late, and the last walk that went into the condition column was the twelfth of February and he entered it on the sixteenth and had believed for four months that he had been exact about it, and he is the only person in eleven streets who has the day of a figure wrong, and there is nowhere to put being right about it.** He did not correct anything: a corrected book is a second book, and a second book is two dates against one fact, and a man in about four years reads both and does not know which one he was carrying. **The thing he got is a knowledge and not a record.** And the other half of the method, in a yard at about half past four, in forty-one words, from a man of forty-six who is stopping: **he has three sentences on the backs of three forms with his name and the date, in the correspondence tray and not a drawer, and a document is a place a person can be asked about** — and *the other half is that a fact is the one thing in this city with no reference, and I have spent seven months building places where nothing can be produced against anybody, and I have built six, and every one of them is a place, and a place has a reference in it the moment anybody looks for it.* Nobody wrote it down and it is in no form, **and a practice is not a place and is not a day.** Field Book: the figure that is a week old; the knowledge and not the record; the eight words said in a corridor and not written; the second tin at twenty-five and the fact that he has said twice he would not write the figure and has written two; the tally line, the fourth time, improved none of the four; the Lark Street grievance not named and not softened in ten days; the page home the hundred and ninety-fourth day, the token the hundred and eightieth day.

### Chapter 125 — A Pin In A Hole In A Road

**REPAIRED AT CR-043: the chapter now prints its own figure in the sequence of things built for one purpose and used for another. A form that can only be wrong in one direction is the wrong shape for a boundary, and that is the ninth in seven months and the first of the nine that is wrong in a direction rather than short of a column.**

**The boundary scene, on this side of the fence, and the boundary grew.** Saturday the sixth of June, about ten in the morning, a hundred and forty feet from the corner, alone, the third time since the twenty-eighth of April. *A boundary is a place where two people agreed to have stopped* — written down four times since May and not a fifth. The corner end is where it was. **The far end is eleven inches further away than it was five weeks ago.** A gang of four had been patching the carriageway since half past eight, and the road peg he drove himself on the second of May is in nine inches of new tar and is straighter than he put it in, and the man of about twenty-six said, unasked, that they take them out and put them back and do not write down which ones, because there are about nine on this side of the river and every one of them is somebody's idea of where a thing was, and **in about nine years there will be a hole in this road with nothing in it and a man will put one in and not know what for**, and that it is not theirs, it is not anybody's, the road is the council's, the council does not know it is there and is not going to know, and the hole will be there after the tar and after him. **Milo Rook did not ask him anything else, the second time in two months he has not asked a man of twenty-six a question, and he wrote the two distances in his own book against the date and nothing else.** The finding: **the hundred and forty feet is a hundred and forty feet and eleven inches.** The gutter peg has not moved a tenth of an inch in ten weeks and could not have, because a man of fifty-four said in May that the water takes it out twice in four years and puts it somewhere else. **The water has not been here since February. A gang of four with a kettle moved the boundary by eleven inches, in the direction of the fence, and it is correct, and there is nobody to enter it, and there is no box in this city for a hundred and forty feet and eleven inches — and the standing sentence that a form cannot be wrong in a direction has been made wrong by a kettle, and the foreman who said it is not going to be told.** The plate is sixty-one days in the grass, the ground has gone from soft to hard, it is not going to be lifted. The standing condition on the fence is unchanged and not improved: *do not go over the fence, and not because I have told you not to.* **One new thing, and it is not a fence thing: the far side is being cut, in June, on a Wednesday, by somebody who is not the works and not the council and is not anybody this district has a name for.** And the two ends: one is a corner in a street because somebody built a street there, the other is a fence because somebody put one up, **neither of them is an agreement, both of them are a boundary, and a boundary does not have to have anybody in it to keep working.** Field Book: the two numbers at the top of the entry and the finding underneath, which is the reverse of four hundred entries; a kettle that moved a boundary; a pin that is the most durable thing in the district and is not a record; ten routes, nine crossings, the token the hundred and eighty-first day and the fourth time in five weeks he did not think about it, and he has stopped writing that clause down; **a practice is not a place and is not a day, and a boundary is not a fourth kind of place, and a pin is not a seventh column.**

### Chapter 126 — A Quarterly Thing Arrives Twice

**REPAIRED AT CR-043 in three places. (a) The twenty-fourth of June is a Wednesday and is thirty-four days after the twenty-first of May, and the chapter no longer calls it eight days after the first request or a Monday, in either the narration or the Field Book. (b) The one sentence of live meta-language in the batch — *this fortnight is the fortnight the volume is going to end on* — is gone from first-person narration, and the escalation now has an in-world reason. (c) The wrong-shape count is the tenth, and the plate in the grass is not miscounted.**

**The escalation, and it is a document, and it arrives twice, and both copies are in one file.** Monday the eighth of June, about half past eleven. **The envelope comes back with a date stamp across the front and nothing else on the outside of it** — not *returned*, not *not stated*, no note, no initials, no letterhead, the flap sealed again with a wafer. Inside it, on the authority's own paper, **the same form**: CAR 214 — LINE THREE. RETURN OF SERVICE PARTICULARS, quarter ending the thirtieth of June, item 6 the number of persons carried on the working of 07:52 off Crown Heights on **Tuesday the ninth of June**, reply requested by **the twenty-fourth**. The finding, which took him until Tuesday morning to write: **item 6 on the December return is the working of the sixteenth of December and item 6 on the June return is the working of the ninth of June, and neither mentions the other, and there is no column for *the same section was not completed last quarter*, and they are in the same file in the same drawer in the same room, eleven weeks apart, and a person who opens that file in about four years gets both of them and both of them are correct.** **A quarterly thing arrives twice. A return is not a record of a quarter. It is a form issued at the front of one and closed at the back of it, and the closing of the first one produced a stamp and no correspondence, and the issuing of the second one did not require anybody to have answered the first one, because the form has no memory. A form that had memory would be a file, and a file is a place a person can be asked about.** A works clerk of forty-six read both sheets and said *it is a return* and then said the half of it that is true, and added that he has had four chances to give a number and none was taken. And the real escalation, which is not a form: **the number is available.** The count for the ninth of June is in a tray in a shed, on nine inches of paper with a hole in it for every person that gets on, and the district could answer in about four minutes and it is the easiest thing this district has ever been asked for. **It is not going to, and the reason is not the line of the twelfth of January and is not the shield and is not the gag: a number that has been put on a return once is a number somebody will ask for twice, and the second time it is not for the return; a roll is not a return and there is no column on nine inches of paper and there never was; a machine counts it and a person does not; and if this district answers one item then in about four years there is a woman of forty-five being asked to produce a roll in a room, in front of somebody, by a man with a box.** And: **the twenty-fourth of June is eight days after the date the first one asked for, and it is a Monday, and there is nothing on any calendar in this city for it, and this district has not had a date since the fourteenth of April, and a district that has a date is a district with a chair, and the list for the fifth of May was six items long and this district was not one of them.** He told a man of forty-one on no list at all, in nine words, at a gate, on the Tuesday, because a man on no list is the only kind of person a thing with no form can be said to. Field Book: two item sixes; the quarter closed and the waiting stopped and nobody told; a form with no memory; the number available and not given; **a fourth part under the third part of the standing question that he has never been down to and is not going to in June and is not going to describe**; three things under a brick; the run number unmoved; the page home the hundred and ninety-seventh day and the token the hundred and eighty-third day.

### Chapter 127 — Thirty-Nine Minutes

**REPAIRED AT CR-043 in six places, all of them the same class. (a) The yard scene is Thursday the eleventh of June, not Wednesday the tenth, in the narration and in the Field Book, and the fourth months off the twenty-second of December is seven weeks past, not two months. (b) The cost is paid at ten minutes to five on a Thursday, not on a Wednesday. (c) The gang is at half past seven, not a quarter past. (d) The twenty-fourth of June is thirteen days off and a Wednesday, not eight days off. (e) The man of forty-six's phrase about a box and the foreman's phrase about a length are still two phrases and are not joined. (f) The phrase *the thing nobody in this district has said out loud in two months* is now seven months, which is the volume's own measure of how long he has been here.**

**A refusal that is correct, and a cost that is not curable, and neither of them is paid by Lena Marr or by the woman of sixty.** Wednesday the tenth of June and Thursday the eleventh. The second side has its **tenth** blank on the Wednesday and is right, and nobody in eleven streets stood in the weather, and the grass on the far side of a fence is being cut at the weekend by somebody who is not the works. **The round goes on Mondays and Thursdays and there is no round on the Wednesday, and everything else in the chapter happened on the Thursday, which was a walk day as well.** The round was thirty-nine minutes late: seven stops, a handcart through the quay section gate, and at about one o'clock the gate was against a lorry with a chain across it and a man in a yellow jacket on the other end who did not know the district and could not have. **The round is not late on the sheet. The sheet is who does what and on which day, it is not a clock, there is no column on that piece of paper or on any piece of paper in this city for a round that was late, and a thing that is not on the sheet is not a fault of the sheet.** Then the second refusal in two days, and neither of them was him asking: he took the return for the quarter ending the thirtieth of June to a counter and **she would not have it behind this counter**, because **a document behind this counter makes this counter a repository, and a repository is a place, and I have three pieces of paper face down under here that a woman counted out loud in a yard in April and said *it is three*, and if a tram authority's return lives under this counter then the three are in a place with a return in it, and a thing in a place can be chased.** And then the finding, in front of four returns: **you left yours where it can be taken, I am not going to bring mine where it cannot, and those are not the same decision, and one of us is going to be wrong about it in about four years and I do not know which and I am not going to find out in a minute and a half.** At about ten past four, a man of fifty-four with a clipboard: **two months past four months.** Nobody has asked him and nobody in this district is going to ask him and that has been done on purpose and it is the yard's doing and not his and not Milo's. **A thing that has never been asked about is not protected. It is just not yet asked about. And in about four years a man with a box is going to ask me, and I am going to have to say yes, and the yes is going to be about a length and not about a morning, and I have had two months to think about which of those two I am going to answer and I have not decided.** **And Milo Rook did not count the words, and noticed at about ten to five that he was not counting, and wrote that down the following night and not the afternoon, and it is the smallest thing that has happened to him in seven months and nobody in this district knows it happened and the not-telling is the eighth of his own habits and none of the eight is a resolution.** And the cost, said out loud in full for the first time, by a man of forty-one on no list at all: **a round is carried by a postman and a postman is on a form and the form wants a depot and a depot is an address, and this district has no rateable unit and no address and has not had one since the eleventh of October six years ago, so the district cannot have a second man on its round, and that is not an injustice, and nobody has done anything wrong, and it is a design, and it is being paid at about ten minutes to five on a Thursday by a boy of seventeen with a handcart, and there is no column for it and the sheet is right.**

### Chapter 128 — The Last Ordinary Day

**REPAIRED AT CR-043 in eight places. (a) The plate decision is dated the first of June, carried and not improved, and Nell Crale's asking on the twelfth did not make it into a decision. (b) The gang is at half past seven on the Monday and the Thursday and does not walk on a Friday, because a Friday is a rack day, and the twentieth walk is now stated on the page as Thursday the eleventh. (c) The round is thirty-nine minutes late on a Thursday. (d) The woman of sixty-one finishes her four returns at the water clinic dispensary, not in four rooms above a fitter's shop. (e) The man of forty-one's nine words are the eighth, which ascends. (f) The page home reads two hundred and first, not hundred and first. (g) The twenty-fourth of June is a Wednesday and is a reply date on a return for the quarter ending the thirtieth, not a Monday and not the quarter's last day. (h) The Field Book now marks the tension between the division's rule about a hand-entered crew and a printed row, and says where the rule stops: it stops at a hand, a printed row is a plan and a plan is not a record, a car did go out on the sixteenth of December, and what did not happen is that it got to the end of the working — and the end of the working is a place that is not on the diagram.**

**The fortnight's end, the last ordinary working day of the district's year, and something given up on purpose and not recovered.** Friday the twelfth of June. The rack at ten past ten and **the second tin at twenty-six**, the sixth week running he has written the figure, and he is not going to write it again and knows it the way he knows the other things. Then an ordinary Friday in order and none of it a decision: the gang at a quarter past seven with two dates on a sheet and the condition column empty on both (**the twentieth walk since the first of April with an empty condition column**), **and the round did not go, because a round is a Monday and Thursday round and has been since the second of January**, the second side out at half past nine and correct, five figures transferred with a pen at about eleven in a room with a machine in it, a letter about a length into the Friday post at about one, two columns read out of six at half past two, a gate at half past four, a doorway at half past five. **That is the whole of what this district produces in a day, it has been producing it since the second of January, and a district that cannot produce a document is a district that cannot be instructed, and a district that cannot be instructed has to be in the street in the morning.** At half past five, in a doorway, **Nell Crale with a hand barrow: *somebody should tell the works that there is a plate off a fence* — and I have not asked you what it was and I am not going to, and this is the one thing I am after you about and it is not what it was.** He did not tell the works. And the resolution, which is the last thing he gave up in six months and he gave it up on purpose: **it is now a decision and not a refusal, and he knows the difference — a refusal cannot be produced, a decision can; a refusal is a thing a person did or did not do and there is nothing on any paper in any building in this city that says he declined to tell the works about a plate, and a decision is a thing a person intends, and a thing a person intends can be described by whoever asks him what he intends, and what he intends is that the plate is never reported, the works is never told, and nobody in this district ever says out loud that it was one of the eleven, and that is final, and it is not because he has found out it was not one of them, and it is not because nobody has asked, and it is because there is nowhere to put the answer and the only place it could go is a form and a form is a document. Nobody has been told he has decided it.** **For seven months he has been looking for a place where a thing cannot be produced against anybody and he has built six and every one of them is a place, and this one is not a place, it is a fact about a man, and a fact about a man is the easiest thing in the world to get hold of.** And then the standing shape of the volume's last fortnight: the plate being cut on a Saturday; **the twenty-fourth of June, a Monday, a date about a car and not a chair**; no second outlook in six months and a man of fifty-one who cannot tell whether the next one does not exist or whether it exists and has not been sent; three nils about a place entered as nil on the ground that there is nobody to write down what happens in it, and not a fourth. Field Book: the ordinary Friday; the plate, given up, as a decision and not a refusal, one of four and not five; **the run number, where it has got to, which is nowhere, and the standing thing a clerk said in April, and one thing left in this district that nobody has looked at and he is not going to write in this book that he is going to look at it, because that would be a plan and a plan is a reason**; the seven walks and the four Fridays and the ten blank Wednesdays and the plate at sixty-seven days; the page home the hundred and first day, the token the hundred and eighty-seventh day, ten routes, nine crossings, the tally unopened, a sound and a taste, **a woman of sixty-eight on nothing added zero and the count at eight.**

### Chapter 129 — Four Feet From A Desk

**REPAIRED AT CR-043, and it is the most important repair in the batch. As first written the chapter put two questions into Milo's mouth in the depot office — whether Hollowgate is on the diagram, and what Hollowgate is — answered both, and then had him and the Field Book and Chapter 130 and three state files all say he had asked the clerk nothing at all. The moral of the volume's close was contradicted by its own scene. Both questions are now out of his mouth: he has them, he holds them for about a second and a half, and he does not ask either, and Hester Coombe volunteers both answers because she can see he is not going to, which is the batch's method doing the work the scene needed. The chapter now says he asked nothing, that there were eight questions, that two were in his mouth and six never were, and Chapter 130 and the state layer agree with it. Two smaller repairs in the same chapter: the margin is no longer dated by absence — the clerk says she will not say whose ink it is or when it was done and will not work it out from what the page has not got on it — and the page home reads two hundred and fourth.**

**The working timetable, and a person who was not sent, and the run number moving one step because of them.** Monday the fifteenth of June. At about half past eight he picked the card up from under a brick, read four characters off the front, and put it back. **The line on the front of it was not taken off.** Three lines on the back, the third in a clerk's hand. He did not go to the timetable division, did not ask anybody anything, sent nobody anywhere, and **the division has not said its sentence once since the twenty-fourth of April, and the sentence is still true, and it is in a book, and it will keep.** At about ten, a corridor in Crown Heights, and the relationship's ninth week: **she is not going to say anything else about the drawer, not this week and not in June, that is not her being cold, and she has stopped requiring anybody in this district to know things**; *it is the ninth week and you have not told me about the season and you are not going to and I am not going to ask, and I want that in a corridor in June in exactly the words I am using*; **he has not asked her about the seventh of January since the fifth of May and has stopped counting and started dating, which is worse, because a date is a column**; **you are going to keep doing that, I am not going to ask you to stop and you are not going to stop and there is no column for either of us, and that is the whole of what this is and it is not going to improve in a year and I have stopped waiting to see whether it does.** Then the work: two returns in three weeks and he told her neither, and she is a party to a proceeding four hundred yards from that office and **there is no column on a return for a party**, and he had not put it in his own book with a date on it because it has a date on it, printed, in the wrong column. **That is correct and it is a very cold way to be correct, and it is the first thing in six months he has done that she would not have done, and she is not going to be warm about it in a corridor, and the warmth is not coming and the not-coming is not a punishment, it is a Tuesday.** And then **Alder Quay, the depot office, about half past eleven.** A depot clerk of fifty-one with twenty-two years in the room counts fares off a tray. **A working timetable is a book on a shelf four feet from where she sits and nobody has ever asked her for it.** Eight columns: *day, time, working, cars, run no, crew, terminates, remarks.* **A terminus is where a working ends. Every working in that book has one, every terminus in that book is on the running diagram, the book is printed from the diagram, the diagram is printed from a list, the list is a hundred and forty years old, and there is no column in that book for a destination that is not on the diagram, and there has never been one, because a column does not check.** She was booking a crew for Monday, **a number is a body of people and a body of people is booked to a working and not to a car, so she had to have the working — that is my job, that is not you and it is not a favour and I have not done it for you and I would not**, and she said on purpose that **nobody has asked her about a morning in December and nobody is going to.** She turned to December and stopped for four seconds. **The 07:52 off Crown Heights, one car, four characters in the run column and four in the crew column, in the type, and that is in the type and it has been in that book since it was printed and it is not on a card under a brick anywhere, and it is in a room, and it is not lost, and it has never been lost, and nobody in the world has to find anything.** And then: **and the terminates column for the 07:52 says Hollowgate.** She said it again when he had not asked. *Have you got it on the diagram.* **No. There is no Hollowgate. There has never been a Hollowgate. I have been in this room twenty-two years and I know every terminus in this book, and it is in the column that only ever holds a place that is on the diagram, and there is a Hollowgate in that column in the type, and the type has been in that book since before I came.** And: **the terminus column takes what it is given, and that is a thing about a column and not about a place.** **He asked her nothing.** Not the type, not a reset, not whether the working ran, not whether the book was printed or typed or written, not whether the diagram ever had it and lost it, not whether anybody in a building about a mile away had ever been asked — **because a clerk who is asked about a morning in December becomes one of about four people in this city who have been asked about a morning in December, and the four of them were not in that building, and I am not going to be a fifth of anything.** And at the door, in nine words, unasked: **there is a thing in the margin of that page** — *it is not mine, it was not there in December and it was there in January, I have not read it and I am not going to, I have not told anybody and I am not going to, and if it is a woman's handwriting then I am not going to be the one who says so, and neither is the timetable division, and neither is the man from the round.* And he did not ask. Field Book: the eight columns and the type; **HOLLOWGATE**, spelt out, unglossed; nine minutes and no questions; the reason in the margin, not copied and not asked about; the page home the hundred and fourth day and the token the hundred and ninetieth day; the standing conditions of the district, none moved; the pairs at twenty-seven; and the standing position.

### Chapter 130 — Both Of Them Took What They Were Given

**REPAIRED AT CR-043 in three places, none of which touches the image. (a) The stop-boarded column on the carbon was filled in on a Monday morning; the sixteenth of December was a Tuesday, and it now says Tuesday and says the column was filled in as they got on, one at a time. (b) The names in the actual margin were dated *on a Tuesday in December*, which dated them by a weekday coincidence that Ch 129 had already dated by silence; they are now dated only by the working whose page they are in, and the chapter says a document's date is not established by what it is silent about. (c) The page home reads two hundred and fifth.**

**THE VOLUME'S FINAL IMAGE, and it is the last chapter of Volume 02.** Tuesday the sixteenth of June. **The sixteenth of December was a Tuesday, and that is a fact about a calendar and it is the only thing about the two dates that is the same, and he did not plan it and did not choose it and has written it in one place only.** He wrote on Monday the fifteenth that he was not going to look at that page and did not write down that he was going to, **because a plan is a reason**, and he did not write it down and he went, and both of those are true. Alder Quay, half past ten. **The round does not go on a Tuesday and has not since the second of January and is not going to; the second side is correct; there is a blank on it for Wednesday and the blank is right.** The carbons were out on the desk, face up, in a tray, in a wooden box, about nine hundred a year, which is where a depot clerk's carbons are at half past ten on a Tuesday. **She did not stop him, did not help him, said nothing about any of it, and he asked for nothing and touched nothing.** The book on the shelf, four feet away; December a third of the way back; the sixteenth a page with columns and a wide ruled margin headed **actual**, **which is where a clerk writes what happened against what was booked, and it is the only part of that book where a person writes, and a person writes in it about four times a day for nineteen years, and it is the right shape for a time and not for anything else.** The 07:52, one car, four characters in type in the run and crew columns, a light engine and a two-car set to a gate at a quarter to eight which takes nobody, and the terminus column saying **HOLLOWGATE**. And in the margin, in ink, in a small upright hand, down about eleven inches of ruled lines, **thirty-one names.** Then the proof, and it is the only four minutes of the volume that is an answer: **they are in order, and the order is the order the people got on.** The carbon for that working was face up in the tray — the depot keeps the carbon, the conductor keeps the sheet, the six columns are *time on, stop boarded, fares, alighted, run no, conductor*, the stop-boarded column was filled in by hand on the length on the Monday morning one at a time, **it has thirty-one names in a hand, and it is not the same hand.** Thirty-one names in a conductor's hand on two feet of carbon, and thirty-one names in a different hand in a bound book on a shelf, **in the same order**, and the order is the order a woman of thirty-eight said out loud about nine hundred times a day and a hole went through nine inches of paper for every one of them, and the roll is right and does not know who one of them is and that is not a defect and she said so before anybody could. **So the order in the margin is the order of the holes, and there is no instrument in this city that could have made that order up, and nobody has ever been asked for it, and there is no column anywhere for it, and no reference and no date, and the carbon has a line at the foot signed N. Bray and the margin has nothing at the foot of it at all.** Then the sentence: ***A destination that does not exist, and the passengers' names are real, and both of those are in the same book on the same page, and they got there the same way, which is that a column takes what it is given.*** The terminus column is built for a place on the diagram and took one that is not on the diagram. The actual column is built for a time and took thirty-one names. **It is a bound book of columns, printed from a diagram, on a shelf, four feet from a desk, in a room, with a person at the desk who was not sent and was not asked and has twenty-two years in the room. It is not lost. It has never been lost. Nobody has to find anything and nobody has anything to find. A working timetable was built for a working day and it has never once been asked to be anything, and it took two things that are not the right shape and it kept both of them.** And the eight sentences that keep it from being anything else, said in his own head standing up at a quarter to eleven: **a destination that does not exist is a fact about a place and not about a person; there is nobody in it; nobody came back; thirty-one people went home out of the river core on the afternoon of the twenty-second of December and they are at home now and they do not know and nobody in this district is going to tell them and there is no form on which anybody could; the names are real because thirty-one people got on a car and the roll punched thirty-one holes and a woman said the stops out loud and a second hand wrote them down in the only place open, and the two match, and a match like that cannot be arrived at by anybody who is making something up; nobody inherits that page and there is no column in that book for who maintains it; it is not a remnant, not a place, not a length, not a row, not a boundary; Hollowgate is not a place that anybody has to be responsible for and the thirty-one are not a class and the four characters are a crew and not a car; the printed part is the false part and the handwritten part is the true part.** At about a quarter to twelve, the only thing she said all morning, four words: *you did not ask me anything* — and then: **there is nothing in this room that anybody is entitled to and there has not been since before you were born, and if you ever come in here and ask me one thing about December I will put the book in the drawer and I will not do it twice.** And the one line that is not about any of it, nine words, given to nobody: ***The line was made up. The names were not.*** And the Field Book, at about half past eleven at night: **fifty-two nights, the list reads twenty-seven and read twenty-seven on the twenty-fifth of April, an interval of one day is not a pair whether or not either of the two days has anything on it**; the four minutes; the page is not a haunting, it is a column that took what it was given; **all the standing conditions of the district gathered on one page of a book in a coat so that there is one place in this city where a person can find them together, and it is not called a document because it does not leave that room**; what he is in June — ten routes, nine crossings, the tally unopened since the thirteenth of January and not going to be looked at in June, the page home the hundred and fifth day blank with four marks in four hands and none of them his, the token cold, a sound and a taste with nothing on either; **a woman of sixty-eight on nothing added zero across ten chapters and the count at eight, and the woman on the fourth floor of Cedar Court is not in any of it, so that the two sixty-eights cannot be folded**; a boundary of a hundred and forty feet and eleven inches; a pin in a hole in a road; eleven men and a clipboard and nobody in this city has ever asked a gang what a length was like; two money totals that disagree at the shilling and are not going to be made to agree; nine doors and no names; a coffee man who does not know that four people know; a foreman two months past four months who has not been asked and has not given anybody that silence; a page on a bottom shelf with a name struck through and no date and no reason — **and the eight of his own, in order, six places and two sentences, the eighth of them on a table in his own office where anybody can walk in and pick it up, and he is going to draw another one in about four months and he is not going to be able to help it, and the seventh column is the only thing he is good at and every time he gets to be good at it somebody else has to hold the paper, and in seven months he has not found another way and he has stopped looking and the not-looking is not a method** — and the last line of the book and of the year of it: ***the writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything, and that is a list of about fourteen other people and not one of them has been asked to be on it.***

---

# Volume 03 — Chapter summaries, Chapters 131–140

### Chapter 131 — A Sentence Where The Machine Prints A Dash

**Wednesday the seventeenth of June. VOLUME 03 OPENS ON A PIECE OF PAPER.** A **district returns card** comes off the same machine as the other eight hundred and something a month, in a room in Crown Heights with a man of about fifty in it who has been there nineteen years, and the card is for **MIREWARD**: *rateable units nil, returns received nil, reason —.* **And then, in the reason column, where the machine prints a dash, there are six words in a hand: *Asked. Nothing came.*** The man of about fifty reads it for about four seconds and **puts it down face up, which is not what he does**, and says he is not arguing about it in the room and that the district is not going to be told whose hand it is. **It is the first sentence anybody has ever written in that column, and it is not a reason for a nil. It is the reason a person is still asking, and every other reason in that column in this city is a reason that nothing is wrong.** The machines are still printing: four of them, toward documents that are not going to be printed, and none has been told to stop, **because being told to stop is a document and this is the district that cannot produce documents.** At half past six in a yard, about nine people, no coffee, he says it in about eleven seconds and then does not improve it; *somebody has to go*, and he agrees, and **nobody in this district is going, because nobody in this district has been told to.** And the **eleventh blank Wednesday** on the second side: the sheet is right about it because the only person who stood in the weather here was a man of about sixty at a shed at Cardew Docks — **and today there is a second reason the sheet is right, and it is a different kind of reason, and a district entered as nil is a place this city's paper cannot see.** At half past four at a gate, the man of forty-one's **ninth nine words**: *You have to go yourself, and you know it* — and the rest of it, that he has been the one not testing it since April, and if anybody is ever sent from that gate then it becomes a place somebody is sent from and he is not going to be the reason that happens before he is forty, and **Milo Rook is the fourth person in this district to be told a thing about his own handwriting.** Field Book: six words, not written down; the standing question's first part with a sentence in it for the first time; the blank with two reasons; the man of fifty-eight in the yard at ten past eight with a rod and a chain and a pad, saying so before he was asked, and Milo Rook going to have to decide whether to ask him about the pad four months after he decided not to. **Page home 206, token 192, pairs 27, plate 72 days.**

### Chapter 132 — Four Lines About Nine Feet Of Drain

**Thursday the eighteenth of June, and the twenty-second walk since the first of April.** Percy, fifty-eight, at the mouth of the yard at ten past eight with a rod and a chain in a bag and a pad under his arm. **He has gone out into Mireward on the first dry week of every summer since 1974, and the pad goes into a drawer in Crown Heights from this year back to about 1969, and nobody has ever asked him for one.** He says all of it before he is asked, and says three times in three different ways that **he is not an office and a man who goes out in a summer and writes four lines about a drain is not an office.** Four hours each way, no bus, the last three miles a raised path with a gate that is not locked **and there is nothing to lock it against.** And then, on the path at about eleven: ***"They put that district down as nil. Every week. Four years I have had that card. And a place is not stable because nothing has happened in it. A place is stable because there is nobody writing down what happens in it."*** He has said it to about four people in forty-one years, two on his way and one in a pub, **and I have now said the same sentence in my own book since February out of a blackboard in a drainage section and neither of us knows the other said it and I am not going to tell him.** The pad has four lines: a date; about nine feet; a water mark, which is the only one that changes and was higher this year; **and a name, the same name for forty-one years, which is not his own, and he has never been asked and is not going to say.** The **refusal**, in four seconds: ***if you put me in a column I have to have a deputy*** — a thing that happens once a year cannot be handed to a man who is sixty-eight, the second one will not have been out in a February, he will not know which mark is which year, and in about eleven years there will be two names in a pad and no way of telling which line belongs to which man. **No form in this city on which to thank a man for not being on a form.** The thing he gives, in a car park with the engine off, in nine words, and it is not a favour: *I will take you out there. Tuesdays. Same as me* — **and then he corrects himself, because it is not Tuesdays, it is the first dry week, it is once a year, and he is telling him the day. There is no bus and there has not been one since about 1961, because a bus wants a place to stop and a place to stop is a rateable thing.** And the cost, which is the whole of it: **one pad, forty-one years, not copied, no schedule, no shelf number, and a man of about forty-one with the key who has never asked and retires in about four years, and he will not have it copied because a copy of a pad is a copy of a man's handwriting and nothing in this city can say which pad was written by the man who was there.** Field Book: four lines and a name; the refusal and the diagnosis; the nine words and the four hours; **and the fact he noticed on the way out — the eleven streets have no address either, and eleven streets has a bus that comes to the end of the street, and he has not once in seven months put those two facts next to each other, and they have been next to each other the whole time.** Page home 207, token 193, pairs 27, plate 73 days.

### Chapter 133 — Half A Bridge Is A Structure And The Other Half Is A Route

**Friday the nineteenth and Saturday the twentieth of June.** Rack day: the sheet into the rack at ten past ten, the duplicate into a tin, **the second tin on the bottom shelf of the plan room now holding twenty-seven weeks of carbon and one page that is not a gang sheet. A Friday is a rack day and not a walking day**, and there is no column anywhere for a sheet that is in a rack. At a quarter to seven a flatbed from a yard at Cobb Lock puts three people down at the end of Bridge Street and **does not wait.** The last thing in this city before the road stops being a road: a brick deck, three spans, a council parapet on the near side, nothing on the far side but a kerb, **eleven yards of grass**, and a raised path going away south. **A boundary is a place where two people agreed to have stopped, and nobody agreed to have stopped at this bridge: two things stopped, for about the same reasons, and neither of them was a person, and that is a different sentence and he wrote both of them down and has not joined them.** He walks over at about ten past seven — **nobody stopped him, there is no fence and no gate and a kerb you could step over, and it took about nine seconds, and there was nobody on either end to see it happen.** A mower man of about fifty stops in the middle of the deck and will not come further: **this half is mine, that half is not mine and I have not been over it in nine years, and there is a line in the middle of it and it is not painted, it is just where the mowing stops, and there is a different grass, because there was a man used to cut that side and he stopped.** The file, ninety minutes, a quarter of an inch, complete: nine inspections in a hundred and thirty-one years, a load calculation revised twice, **a schedule of works with a column for *side* — every entry says CITY except two, and the two say EXT, and the key says *external — see the drainage board*, and the drainage board was abolished in about 1968 and its functions went to a body called the fen commission, and there is no fen commission on any letterhead in this city.** The woman at the desk: **that half has not been anybody's since I came here and I have been here twenty-one years and I am not going to say one word about the other half, because it is not my file and I do not want it in my file.** The finding, and it is the reason a place can be entered as nil on nine hundred cards a month: **the half that is maintained is the half a lorry can stand on, and the half that is not maintained is the half that people walk on.** He did not write a seventh column, and he noticed, and wrote *did not* next to it with the time against it — one past one. On the Saturday, the woman who runs the landing is thirty-seven and **he does not ask her name until the Sunday and she makes him ask**: **the handrail post is four inches shorter than the one it took out, put in about 1979 by two men from a yard who were not liars, and it is the only thing anybody has ever used to measure that path, and she does not want it fixed, she wants it written down that it is low, because the day a man comes out with a level and puts it right about nine families lose the only thing they have got, and there is no form in this city for a thing nine families use that is not a building.** And the boundary, said out loud at about three on the landing: **it is not a boundary because everybody agrees where the grass stops and nobody agrees about anything else — mine is a place where two people agreed to have stopped and yours is a place where two people stopped, and the water agreed with both of them and the water does not keep minutes.** The smell again in a dry week, and **it is neither the sound nor the taste**, and the time is twenty past two, and the words are *not one of the two*. And Percy, at about four on the Friday, on a bridge: **you do not go there, you go back, and you start from the far end — a ferryman does not navigate to a place, a ferryman navigates from a place, and you have been going the wrong way round for seven months.** The Field Book has **no entry for the nineteenth and none for the twentieth, and the two are not the same kind of nothing: the Friday was a decision, because there is a piece of paper in his coat and he was not going to sit down and write a book entry about a piece of paper; the Saturday was not a decision at all.**

### Chapter 134 — A Column That Wants A Name And There Is Nobody To Put There

**Saturday the twentieth to Monday the twenty-second of June.** Percy came back in his own van on the Thursday and went out again on the Friday, because the first dry week is four days. **Milo went out on the flatbed at a quarter to seven on the Friday, and for four nights he slept in four rooms in a place that has no address, and not one piece of paper came into existence about any of the four nights, and there is no register anywhere that would have a line in it, and that is not a trick anybody played — it is what a place with no address is.** On Sunday he wrote a form headed *why this district is entered as nil* at the top of the district's own two inches, **and then he filled it in, and it is the best thing he has written since the third of June, and it is the same mistake for the eighth time in about six months.** A hundred and ten words, every word true, **and no name in it, because there is no name to put there.** Percy finds it at about seven on the Monday morning on the raised path and names it in about eleven seconds: **you have written a seventh column, and it is about a place. It is the first one you have written that wants a name. Every other one wanted a person to put their name against the why. This one wants somebody to be the reason, and there is nobody, and there has not been anybody since 1969, and you have written a hundred and ten words about a hole in this city where the people who would be the reason are not.** And then the part that is worse: **a column that wants a name and has nobody to be given one is the same thing as a column that wants a name and is refused one, and both of them are a form that wants a person, and for seven months the problem has been that there is a person on the other end who does not want their name on it, and this time there is nobody, and I knew that on Sunday morning before I finished.** And the **ninth resolution, the first one that is not a place: he hands the paper to the person it is about.** Garn Ollerton, on the Sunday afternoon at the bollard, refuses in about nine seconds and then tells him who to ask and makes sure he cannot come back and say she did not tell him — *it is not my right and you know it is not my right, and if I take that card then in about four years there is a woman in a room who says I have never seen it and a man in a room who says I gave it to her, and I am not going to get out from under it by being right.* Percy says no in four seconds in the same voice in the same order as the last one, and gives the whole reason again: **two pads, and one of them with a stranger's hundred and ten words in the bottom of it, and neither of us can tell anybody which one is the record.** **It is in the bottom of a bag with the Field Book and it is the first of the eight kept by a person rather than by a place, and a person is not a custody, and the ninth resolution did not work.** On the Monday at half past seven the gang walked the back length for the **twenty-third** time with both columns empty, **and for the first time in seven months he was not on it and nobody telephoned and Roy Pryce wrote nothing in the hours column and the sheet is right about that as well** — and a length is a third kind of place and it is not a remnant and it cannot be pinned because there is no edge on it, and it remains a distinct kind of place for exactly as long as the people who walk it keep walking it. And at about four on the Sunday, out in the boat to the second, third and fourth landings, Garn Ollerton says the thing the crossing will be built out of, as a job and not a favour, unasked: **I do not go to places, I go from places. There are four of them and the day starts where the boat is. If you want to know whether somebody can get from the fourth landing to your office you do not ask the fourth landing, you stand there and watch them come in, and you go backwards from the boat.** **There are about nine routes on that water and every one was written by a person who was not going to be on it, and there is not one piece of paper about any of them, and a route on paper is a route somebody maintains and a route somebody maintains is a route somebody can be asked about.**

### Chapter 135 — A Schoolroom With No Address And Nineteen Children In It

**Tuesday the twenty-third and Wednesday the twenty-fourth of June.** Coll Sarrowe, forty-four, at a sluice at about seven with a bar in both hands, thirty-one years, and the entire interview for four minutes is the word *water*. **He is the other half of the man of fifty-eight and neither of them knows the other exists: Percy writes down what he finds once a year and nobody reads it, and Coll Sarrowe holds the water down every day of his life and there is no paper in this city with his name on it.** Percy tells him when he is going, once a year, in about March, in a car park, and has since about 1971, and Coll has never asked to go and has never been asked. **The day I stop, it fills in about nine days and then it goes over the road at Cobb Lock and into about eleven houses, and then it is not a question about a bar, it is a question about eleven houses, and a man from the water authority is in a car in about four hours and he does not know anything about drains.** The refusal: **a named ditch keeper is a ditch keeper who can be got rid of. There have been four of us named in this district in thirty-one years and three of them left inside two years, and the one who is still here is the one who is not, and that is not a mystery and it is not a grievance.** Cobb Lock: a hundred and forty people in high water, **no address, and the address is not missing, it was never there** — a school is a rateable thing, a rateable thing needs a street, the village had a street number in about 1911 and it was renumbered for a drain and the new numbers went on a sheet in a drawer and nobody has ever asked for the sheet. Sabra Trewin, thirty-four, nine years: **nineteen on a Tuesday, twenty-one on a Monday, and I do not have a piece of paper with twenty-one on it, and I have never had a piece of paper with any of them on it, and that is not a system of mine, it is the absence of a system, and I have got a roll in my head and it is the only roll of these children that exists anywhere.** They are not on the education roll, not on the relief list, not in a clinic's records, **and if there was a flood tomorrow they would be counted on the back of a bus by a man with a pen, and that would be the first piece of paper ever made about these nineteen children, and it would be made about them on the day, by somebody who does not know any of their names. And if I am on that bus then it is gone.** And the refused address: **a village that has no address is not a place that has been forgotten; a place that has been forgotten was on something and came off it. This was never on anything. Nobody did this to us. There is no order, no sheet, no meeting at which anybody decided it, and there is a drawer in Crown Heights with the numbers in it from 1911, and if somebody went and got it out and put it back we would be a place with a number inside a fortnight, and nobody has ever gone. And it would be a thing that could be taken away again, and we have been un-takeable for about sixty years by accident, and I am not going to be a woman who asks a city to do us a favour.** The smell at twenty to three, **four times written in a working book and no explanation**, and *it is the fen, and it is there in a dry week and everybody over about sixty cannot remember a year without it, and I have never known anybody to put it in writing and I have never known anybody to be asked.* And then the standing question turns over, standing still on a path, in about nine seconds: **a resident witness is a person on a list.** The forecast was issued for a season in which a district with no roll and no recorded loss was entered as nil on the ground that there is no resident witness, and the entry **has never once been wrong about a fact and has been completely wrong about a place for sixty years**, and a district that is not a place cannot be frightened. And the **twenty-fourth of June** — the only date this district has had since the fourteenth of April — went at midnight on Tuesday, and he was eleven miles away, and the round did not go, and nobody in eleven streets stood in the weather, and the twelfth blank Wednesday is right. **The return will be closed with the item standing open, and in about four years somebody will pull that file and find two item sixes and no column anywhere for the difference, and late is a thing that happens to a person.**

### Chapter 136 — A Man Walks Him In And Nine Rings On A Post

**Thursday the twenty-fifth of June. THE TENTH CROSSING.** The gang walked the back length at half past seven for the twenty-fourth time with both columns empty, **and for the second time in a fortnight he was not on it and nobody telephoned.** A lorry comes into Mireward on a Thursday morning to a yard at the north end and does not wait, and there are two motor vehicles a week and a tractor, and the tractor belongs to a man who will not be named. **Ola Petrossian, twenty-three, a nurse, got off it at about nine, nobody asked her, the man of fifty-eight told her on the Saturday in about nine words, and she came on her day off and walked four miles from a yard to get there and is paid by a clinic with no record of where she went.** The four conditions of October, said out loud at about ten past two **to a drain**, in order, in front of about six people: a person walks him in and out and they are not him (**Garn Ollerton, doing her run, not asked, not offered**); a count held by a person and not by a box (**Sabra Trewin, in her head, and she is not writing this one down either**); the time stated before he goes (**in at twenty past two, out by five past three, forty-two minutes, said in advance so that if he is not out there are six people who know**); and the clinic in the morning. **In at 14.20, out at 15.02, forty-two minutes, from the old ford, on a Thursday afternoon in the fourth week of June, in a dry week, in a district entered as nil.** **It was not a street. It has no edge. The far side of it is more of the same.** The ford is bone dry and has been since 1919, and **the water is up to a mark two feet below the top of the parapet, and the mark is a tidemark — somebody stood there and looked at it — and there are three of them and the top one is newer than the bottom one by about nine years.** The ground goes on being the same ground and the water goes on being where the water is and the two of them are together. The smell is about four times what it was outside and **it came from underneath the whole length of it rather than from anywhere, and he has lost a sound and a taste and this was neither of them.** And at about four hundred yards along, a post with **a school bell on it, five inches across the mouth, seven feet up, no school attached, and nineteen marks cut into the post low down in a row, two of them still pale on the Wednesday.** **It had been rung nine times. Not while he was there. He stood under it for about eleven minutes with his watch out and it did not ring, and he wrote all nine times down afterwards and could not put a ring on any of them. He did not touch the post. He did not go and look at the marks from underneath. There was nothing else in forty-two minutes and he came out, and this is the first of the ten he has been in and out of that he has come out of with less than he went in with.** On the bank: *you are two minutes over* — and then the half of it that matters, that she has been sitting there since twenty past two doing a job she does every Thursday, and he did not ask her and she would have said yes and she is glad he did not, **and those are two different things and I am not going to mix them up in a yard.** And Sabra Trewin: **twenty minutes' worth is a pair, and I did not write it down, and I am not going to, and there are nineteen marks on that post and I put four of them in on the Wednesday and I am not going to tell a man in a coat which four.** The sound went again on the Wednesday at about ten past eleven at a clinic counter, four seconds, and came back, and did not stay. And **one Atlas residue in ten chapters, eight words, in his own book, on a bank, given to nobody and to be given to nobody: *A dry week is a promise about water.*** Field Book, **seven days off the eighteenth**: **the list on the facing page reads TWENTY-EIGHT, two sixes, seven threes, one nine, four twos, three fives, one eight, one twenty-five, THREE sevens, six fours; it had not moved in seventy-seven days and it moved tonight, and it moved because he was asleep for six nights in a place with no address with the book in the bottom of a bag and did not open it once, and a pair is a fact about where he was and not about what happened.** Page home 214, token 200, plate 80 days.

### Chapter 137 — Nine Tides Is Not Nine Years

**Friday the twenty-sixth of June, and the whole of the recovery.** Rack day: the sheet into the rack at ten past ten in a room he was not in, **the second tin now on twenty-eight**, and *the office does not keep a list of who was there.* At about eleven that morning, on the bank of the drain at the old ford, with a woman of thirty-seven and a folding rule, she makes him put his own number down twice: **that is not nine years, that is nine tides. A man does not cut a mark because a year has gone by; he stands there when the water is at a place and cuts a mark, and the water comes about four times a year, and if he has cut three in a row and the top one is nine down the list then it is two years and a bit, and a bit is a spring tide or an autumn one.** And then the whole of it: **you wrote a number of years on a mark that has never had a year on it because you have a book in a coat with four marks in four hands in it and you are so used to dates that a number without one looks wrong to you. The file says 1911 and the file is right about 1911 and the file says nothing whatever about these marks, because the file is on the other side of the water.** **THE FIRST FINDING IN SEVEN MONTHS THAT RUNS THE OTHER WAY: every conclusion this district has produced has been that something was going to be found out about a person, and this one is that a thing is safe because there is nothing to find.** Two sets of marks about ninety feet apart, cut by two different hands, recording the same water on the same days, **and a route that cannot be produced cannot be asked after, and that is the only protection any route in this city has ever had.** He would like to say that in a yard; *no* — **it is a perfectly good sentence, and it is about a mark on a wall that I could not tell a stranger about, and there are about four people alive who know what I mean by the low one on the path, and if the two of us start saying that a bell and a wall and a post are a route then in about four years somebody writes it down, and a thing that is written down is a route somebody maintains, and the day after that it is a route somebody can be asked about, and I have watched that happen to about nine routes on that water and I am not doing it to two marks and a bell.** He came back on the flatbed at about four. **And the crossing was read out in a yard at about half past five, to about nine people, with the tally there — the eighth time, and the fourth time in a place that is not this district's yard. The paper is Hettie Brann's, the pocket is Dr Sen's, the tally has ten lines, and he has not looked at it since the thirteenth of January and is not going to in July.** She makes him say the number, the date, the times, who walked him, who held the count, and the four conditions, and **nobody asked the nurse.** At the gate at about half past four, the man of forty-one's **tenth** nine words, and it is not a refusal: *I was not there. I am not going there* — **and he said the other half of it before Milo could get to the second sentence of his own, which is that he has not been south of that bridge since he was a boy and he is not going to start at thirty-eight.** And Garn Ollerton, at about ten past twelve, unasked: **you will want to go back in. Then do it from here. Not from your end. You do not know where you are until you know where you are going to be, and that is the only thing about you that is not a document. And I am not going to take you. Not this week. And that is not a rule and I have not got a reason you would print.** Field Book: **a pair is a fact about where I was and not about what happened**; **the first conclusion in seven months that runs the other way**; the count of nine words is ten and **I could not have told you what proportion of them were refusals, and that is the difference between a number and a column, and I have gone six months without noticing it**; page home 215, token 201, the condition column empty for a hundred and thirty-four days, the reason column for the fourteenth week, plate 81 days.

### Chapter 138 — A Sentence With A Number On It

**Saturday the twenty-seventh and Sunday the twenty-eighth of June. THE ESCALATION, AND IT IS A DOCUMENT, AND IT TOOK FOUR SECONDS OVER A COFFEE RING.** The card comes off the same machine on the Saturday morning **in the second run** — and the second run exists because the man of about fifty put the first card down face up, and face up is not a state a card is in, and once a week at about four in the afternoon the day's cards are re-run so that a face-up one goes back through the machine in case an operator has left it the wrong way up, **and there is a line at the foot of that run sheet that says *re-run — face up only*, and the line is on the form, and the form is about a printing error and not about a district.** So there are two cards, and one has no number and **the other one has a number in the top left corner, and the number is a reference in a series that runs from something like 41,000 upward, and about four hundred of those go out of that room a week.** The man of about fifty: **I have put a card face up twice in nineteen years and one of them was a card for the river core that had a coffee ring on it, and I did that in about 1981 and I have not done it since, and I did it on Wednesday and I would like to go back to about 1981.** In a corridor at ten past one: **you have told me about the first one and you have not told me about the second one, and the reason you have not told me is that the second one is the one with a number on it, and you worked that out about four hours ago and you have been carrying it ever since.** And the eleven seconds, which are his and which she says he did not need her for: **a sentence is a thing a person can be sent to look at, and a sentence with a reference on it is a thing a person can be asked for, and the asking is done in a form, and the form goes in a file, and the hand is not on the form, and the hand was on the paper, and the machine took the hand over, and there is nothing in this city that can make a machine give a hand back.** And then the thing this is for, said once and not twice: **there is a line in a return that I reconcile on the last Thursday of every month which has nothing to do with you and nothing to do with a tram, and I have looked at it, and I am not going to tell you what it says, and I am telling you that I have looked at it.** He asks what kind of line it is. **That is the second time, and I said once.** In a yard at half past six, about nine people, no coffee, forty seconds: **asked, nothing came** — said twice, and he says he is not going to say it a third time, **and if it is on a card with a number on it then the number is the part that matters and the six words are the part that does not.** And the wrong question caught in the yard by somebody who is not him: *who gets asked* — **that is the wrong question and you know it is, the right one is who asks the room for the number, and it will not be anybody in this district, because anybody in this district who asks for it is on the form, and not one of the nine people here has a reason to be on a form in June.** Field Book: **the face-up card is the whole of the mechanism and it is a line at the foot of a run sheet about a printing error, and I have written a great deal in this book about the difference between a thing a person does and a thing an office does and I have never once watched the second one eat the first one in about four seconds.** And: **I did not write the six words in this book, and the reason is that a Field Book is paper and graphite and lives in a coat and this coat goes into rooms, and that is the third time I have done a thing like that in seven months and the three of them have the same shape, and the shape is a place where a sentence is safer than it is in the world.** And: she has not told him a thing in six months and she told him this, and he did not ask a second question, **and if she tells me I am going to be a person who has been told and that is a different column.** Page home 216, token 202, plate 82 days, pairs 28.

### Chapter 139 — A Man Who Is Not Paid For Holding The Water Down

**Monday the twenty-ninth and Tuesday the thirtieth of June.** The gang on the back length at half past seven, the **twenty-fifth** walk since the first of April, both columns empty; the round out at ten past eight and in at about four, one man short since the twenty-eighth of December. And eleven miles away, at a sluice belonging to a body abolished in 1968, **a man of forty-four held a bar in both hands for about an hour and was paid nothing for it.** He is paid by a farm by the hour for ditching, **and two days a month of it is a sluice, and the farm does not know I am doing it and the water authority does not know I am doing it and the sluice does not know I am doing it, and if I do not turn up on a Tuesday in a wet week then the water goes over the dyke at Cobb Lock and it is not a decision anybody took, it is a man who was somewhere else.** And the ratepayer problem, which is the design and is the whole of it: **I am not on anything. A man with no address cannot be a ratepayer, because a ratepayer is a person at an address, and a man with no address is not a person to a form. So the water authority cannot bill me and cannot employ me and cannot write me a letter that would arrive, and I am doing it out of a farm wage, and the farm pays me for the Tuesday and the Tuesday is not a ditch and the farm does not know that either.** **So the cost is not mine. The cost is that it is a Tuesday. And nobody will ever write that down, and in about four years somebody will say the dyke failed, and the dyke did not fail, and there is a bar in it.** And the one rescue in nine years, told on the Friday in about four seconds while he did not stop working: a woman of about thirty and a boy of about nine off a top bank in July, **two hours with a chain and the tractor, and the tractor is not insured to be in that water and I knew that, and the chain came off the second time and I went round it three times and the boy came out on the third and the woman came out on the fourth attempt and the tractor is a tractor.** **Nobody reported it, and there is nothing to report it on: a form for an accident at work and I am not at work, a form for a drowning and nobody drowned, and a form for a rescue, and a rescue asks who they were and where they lived and what their number was, and I have got none of those, and the tractor has a serial number and the tractor is not a person. So it happened, and there is one tractor, and there is a chain, and the day the chain breaks is the day it stops, and I have thought about that more than I have thought about anything.** The refusal that is correct, and it is the man of fifty-eight's fifth nine words and the third that was not about a job: **I leave at six.
