Thursday the twenty-seventh of August, in the records room in Crown Heights, and Milo Rook is not in the room and has not been in it on a last Thursday in about two years.** She reconciles the return on the last Thursday of every month and has done for about two years. **A return is a grid: a row for every district, a cell for every week, a figure where a district returned a figure, and a reconciled return is one in which every cell carries one or the other, and there is no third thing and no blank, because a blank in a cell is not an answer, it is a query, and a query goes down.** She does the counter first, standing up, in about four minutes: a certificate on a permit from 1974, and a copying fee. The eleventh street district is on the fourth page from the bottom. Its row is a district and a set of cells and nothing else, **no address because there is no correspondence address, and no person because there is no person.** She saw the schedule's effect and not the schedule. **The last cell is the week beginning Monday the thirty-first of August and it has not happened yet and it is four days away. She put a dash in it at about eleven, with a pen and not a pencil, in about four seconds, and that is the whole of what she did on the last Thursday of August.** Then four minutes of looking at it. **A dash in a cell is a departmental statement that nothing was received. It is not a statement about the district. It is a statement about the post.** The satchel went out with the works' papers because the works' papers were the only thing in this district that ever weighed anything, and there is no works' paper and therefore no satchel out, and there are two hundred and fifty-two people in a building with no number, one a boy of seventeen and one a woman of sixty on a second landing who is the ninth of twenty-six names, and not one of them is on the post. **Nothing was not received. Nothing was not sent. The form has one mark and the mark does not have a way of saying which of the two it is.** Then the register of correspondents, **about four inches thick, a list of addresses, made to tell a department where a letter goes, and the wrong shape for a district, because a district is not somewhere a letter goes, it is somewhere letters come from**: a district on that register does not receive demands, it generates requests, and a request needs a reply, and a reply needs a name. **She opened it at about ten to twelve with a pen in her hand and shut it ninety seconds later and entered nothing.** The reason, written on the back of a sheet and torn up: **a district that can be written to is a district whose returns are held somewhere, and a district whose returns are held somewhere is a district somebody can ask a card from, and there is one card in this city with a hand on it for the fen country and it has a line through it and a man's name under the line and the re-run is on a Friday at about four.** Nine minutes of versions and none of them safe, including the one that leaves the district on exactly one printed side in the whole of this city as a row that is not going to be maintained. **She did not describe the line, nobody asked her to, and a cell and a line are two different things in a return and paper is not a reason.** The season and the drawer are the same silence and it is the ninth time. At ten past four, in a corridor, the round's bag over his shoulder: **I have put a dash in the last cell… I have known since Wednesday that you would want a sentence in it, and I have not said no to you today, because you have not been in that room, and I am not going to say it out here either.** Then the reason, once, at the speed she reads forms out loud: **if that district ever gets an address then this office holds its returns, and the day this office holds a district's returns is the day somebody can ask this office for a card** — and the card is his, and he cannot take the name off, and **she is not going to be the one who gives the room the address that lets them ask for it.** And then the other thing, unasked for: **because a sentence in that cell becomes a thing this room can be asked for, and about four years from now a man is going to ask this room whether anything was received from that district in the week beginning the thirty-first of August, and this room is going to say yes, and hand him the sentence, and the sentence is going to be true, and it is going to be mine. And a true sentence in my hand is the second time this year that a true sentence in somebody's hand is the whole mechanism, and the first one did not need a hand at all. It was a Thursday and a man with a pen and about a minute and a half.** He did not ask her which Thursday. And then the disagreement, third time, unrepaired, and colder: **it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing that exists** / **it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing a man with a box can ask for in about four years** — **Yes. And I have proved both of those in one morning, at the same desk, with the same pen, and you were not in the room, and I am not going to pretend that either of us is going to win it, and I am not going to be warm with you about it in a corridor.** **Nor am I.** **Good. Go and do your round, you are one man short and you have been one man short since December and the bag is not the district.** And the close: **the fifth cell is the only thing she has written this month and it is a dash and it is wrong, and she has left it, and she is not going to be thanked, and she has not been, and she is not going to be.**

### Chapter 159 — The Last Morning Of A Contract, And A Week That Was Never Printed

**Monday the thirty-first of August, and the last walk of the season, and the forty-third since the first of April, and there is no form in this city for a walk that is not on a sheet.** The plate is a hundred and forty-seven days old, the page home is the two hundred and eighty-first day, the token is cold on the two hundred and sixty-seventh, the condition column is two hundred days, **the second tin went to thirty-seven in a building eleven miles away on Friday morning and it is not going anywhere after that and the tin does not know that and neither does the repair schedule it is filed under**, and a foreman of fifty-four is eighteen weeks and five days past four months and is on a road at half past seven. **The barriers were already out and the road was open and both of those are correct, and a site is not cleared by a date.** The sheet: **a weekly sheet is a printed form and printed forms come off a machine and a machine cannot print a form for a period that is not in a contract.** Roy Pryce had asked, on the Friday, and was told there is no week after the contract, and said, at ten past eight, to nobody in particular, with his thumb on the clipboard: **That is not the office being difficult, that is a machine, and the machine is right, and I have got a gang standing on a road at half past seven on a Monday because a contract says to the thirty-first and the contract does not know what day of the week the thirty-first is.** Nobody answered him. **A sheet is a record of a length of road maintained between two Mondays. It is the wrong shape for a morning, and this morning is a morning, and it happened, and eleven men were on it, and there is nowhere to put it.** And the column: **the condition column is not empty today. The condition column is not there.** He told the gang there was no sheet, which was true, and **the true sentence covered about four of the things that were missing, and a true sentence that covers four things is a sentence that is being used.** The man of about twenty-six was at the north end at eight, asked for nothing, and **is not a fifth of anything.** Then four hours. **The seventy-eight words** — the sentence he did not say on the twenty-fourth, quoted whole — **and on Monday the thirty-first of August, on a road, in daylight, with four hours, I did not say them, and I could not have said them**, and four answers, every one of them half: no form, and a road is not a room and nine people is not a file; anything I said would be about me; a document is a door, and Pike said so in four seconds on a top track and four people have told him what not to do and not one of them has been thanked; and fourth, the one that is on the page — **on Monday the twenty-fourth of August, in a yard, with about nine people in it, Roy Pryce said the whole of the argument out loud, and it was correct, and it is not in any file and it cannot be put in one, and he has said he is not going to say it again. So the sentence exists. It is in a yard, spoken, remembered by about nine people, and that is the only place it is and the only place it can be, and there is nothing for a man with a book to add to it, and the adding would be the only part of it that was mine.** **It cost Roy Pryce and he will never be told and he did not ask and he is not going to** — and the arithmetic of the cost: **the whole of the record of Roy Pryce's last day in charge of that gang is a figure in a tin in a plan room in another building, and a figure in a tin is not a record of a man, and a plate in the grass a hundred and forty-seven days old that nobody has reported, and not one of those is a sentence and not one of those can be put beside a name in a box.** **A yard is the only instrument in this district that has never once turned into a document, and he knew that in June and did not use it and is not going to be able to use it again this year. And that is the sixth thing of the other shape, and the other shape has six in it, and the five that I have been calling my five have five in them, and those are two different lists and I am writing that down now because in about four years somebody is going to merge them and add them up and get a number that means nothing.** He did not go to the low end of Bridge Street: **that is now the fifth time I have not gone and looked at a thing I could have looked at**, and this was the last morning there would be eleven men and a road on that side, and **the not-going is now about a place rather than about a person, and I do not know what the difference is worth and I have decided to stop finding out what it is worth.** Field Book, 23.55: **the promise, broken in writing and not explained** — the end of July was the thirty-first of July, he did not do it then, and on the thirty-first of August he writes that he is not going to, and that is the second time and the second time has cost a day, **and a promise kept in a book and broken in a book is not a promise and is not a break and is a third thing**; **the rule, which runs out tomorrow: I am not going to draw a seventh column on the first of September either, and the reason is that I did not want the column and the twenty-eighth of August was when I found out that I did not want it, and it has taken me three days to admit that the reason I was not going to wait was that I was hoping a month would make it correct, and a rule that runs out is not a release and it never was a stop, and I am not going to be improved by anybody in a yard**; and the pairs, twenty-nine, unmoved, an interval of one day is not a pair, and it is the sixth time.

### Chapter 160 — A Rack With Nothing In It In It, And A Question With A Practice In The First Of Its Parts

**Tuesday the first of September and Friday the fourth, and the block's end, and it is not a cliff.** **On Tuesday the first there was a half past seven on the back length and there was nobody on it.** Page home 282, token 268, plate 148, condition 201, **second tin at thirty-seven**. **The barriers were still out and the road was open and there is a difference between a barrier that is up because men are coming back at half past seven and a barrier that is up because nobody has come, and the two look the same from a bus and there is no vehicle to see it from.** The ordinary day, which was ordinary: the man of about forty sweeping the Row, the fishmonger of about forty-five with her trays out by nine, a man of about sixty with a shed eleven minutes away who is not on the Row on a Wednesday and has not been since before the war, **and it is not a Wednesday, and he does not know that a piece of paper exists and is not going to be told.** And then the sixth thing, and it is not the works: **there has not been a satchel out of eleven streets since Friday the twenty-eighth of August, and there is not going to be another one, and the works are a third of it.** The Friday post was the works' papers because a district with no correspondence address is on no other post; **so the one printed side that has arrived weekly in this district since the middle of January has not arrived, and nobody was told, and there was no notice, and the notice is a printed side that says the works are not to be maintained after the thirty-first of August, and the notice is about the works, and the works are the post, and the post was never mentioned, and nobody in an office about a mile away looked at a calendar, because a printed date does not know what day of the week it is.** **He has not made a form about a printed side, or a cell with a dash in it, or a typed name in a box, or a sheet that was never printed, or a week that was never printed, or this.** Friday the fourth: **the round did not go, because the round is a Monday and a Thursday round and has been since the second of January.** He walked up himself. **The rack is a piece of furniture, about four feet high, open at the front, against a wall, and on Friday the fourth of September there is a rack with nothing in it in it.** He put his hand in it; it was cold; there is a mark on the shelf where a bundle of sheets had been lying, about nine inches from the left edge, about two inches wide, **and it is the only trace of nine months of weekly sheets in this city and nobody put it there on purpose.** **Nobody empties a rack. That is not a decision anybody made. It is a thing that has never had to be done, and a thing that has never had to be done does not have a form, and the absence of a form is not a fault, and there is nothing in this city that says a rack is finished, and there is going to be a rack with nothing in it in it on Friday the eleventh and on Friday the eighteenth and on the Friday after that.** And the man of forty-one on no list at all, with a handcart, not sent, not going to be: *Does anybody empty that.* / **I have been in this office four times a day since the twenty-eighth of December. I have watched that rack. It has been got from and never got from. I am not going to be the man who says which of those it is.** Nine words, no column for the number of times, and nobody thanked him. And the men: **there were eleven men and a foreman on this road twice a week for nine months and more, and as of the first of September the district does not know where they are, and there is no way of finding out, and there is not going to be one — the contract is between the city and a firm, a firm is rateable, a firm has an address and a file and a name and can be written to, and the district cannot, and there are no eleven men and no foreman in the file at all, and the only trace that any of them was ever on that road is a figure in a tin in a plan room in another building, and a figure in a tin is not a record of a man, and two entries in pencil, four words and a figure, on the thirteenth and the twentieth of July, and the line on the front of a boy's blank card under a brick has not been taken off either and there have been three things under that brick since the twenty-first of January. That is not a lament. It is an inventory.** Then the standing question in three parts, **with a practice in the first of them and no form at all**: (a) **it is four bells and a refusal to write them down, and the refusal is the oldest thing I have**; the four set out whole, unnumbered as a pattern; **and the finding that goes with it, unimproved: a practice is the only thing in this district that has a shape no form was ever built to fit, and the reason there is no form for it is not that a form is missing. It is that the woman on the schoolroom step would have to decide how many pulls of the rope is the right number before she could put a figure in a box, and four old women who are dead taught four children that number, and she does not know that she has the right to flatten it out, and I do not know that she has, and neither of us has asked her, and there is no form for *I do not know that I have the right*, and that is not a gap in a form, it is the whole subject.** (b) **there is no second outlook** — unmoved, forty-six days, **nobody has asked for one in writing since May**, the absence unexplained. (c) **seven instruments that are not one**, the seventh being the bell, **a bell cannot tell you a date and a bell cannot tell you a number that matters, and the number at the end of a practice turns it into a rule, and a rule can be broken by a person**; the fourth part under the third has still not been gone down to, and it is the sixth time he has written that sentence. **And the category is named in this book since the thirty-first of July and it has got him a question and not an answer, and there is a street in this city that would settle it in about a day, and he is not going to look at it.** And the close, an inventory and not a lament: **four bells, four people, a practice, no row, a man of forty-one who has watched a rack for two hundred days and will not say whether it is ever emptied, a district on one printed side, a sheet that was never printed, a week that was never printed, a rack that is never finished, and nobody has been thanked.**

## Volume 03, Batch 0004 (Chapters 161–170) — *What The Rain Is Carrying* — state after Chapter 170

### Chapter 161 — A Week In Which The Only Thing This District Has Is An Absence

**Monday the seventh to Saturday the twelfth of September.** The round went out at ten past eight and came in at about four with the bag empty; **it is a Monday and a Thursday round, one man short, the bag has been empty since the twenty-eighth of August, and it is not a walk any more and nobody has called it one.** Page home 288, token 274, plate 154, condition 207 on the seventh; page home 293, token 279 on the twelfth; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty weeks and three days past four months on the twelfth.** The count, in four minutes on the back of a sheet of his own paper, standing up: **seven days since the last walk of the season; ONE rack day that was not a rack day, the fourth, because a rack day is a Friday and a sheet covers Monday to Sunday; THREE Mondays and Thursdays with nobody on them.** *There is no column in this district that wants any of those three numbers.* A district is entered as stable or not stable; a week is walked or not walked; a day is gone or not gone; **nothing in this city can be told that a week went by and there was nothing in it**, and a period with nothing in it is a dash, and a dash is a statement about the post and not about the district, and this district has no cell because it has no return because it has no post. **AN ABSENCE IS NOT A FORM.** He drew the four columns in pencil in about eleven seconds — *Week ending. What was done. By whom. What was found. Why nothing was found.* — **the best one he has ever drawn and the same mistake for the eleventh time in eight months, and the mistake is a column that wants a person, and there is no person in it: there is a week and a nothing.** Tuesday the eighth: the barriers up, the road open, the rack cold with a mark on the shelf that has not changed, **and there is no instrument in this city for a mark that has not changed.** The drawer and the bus: **a sheet of 1911 street numbers in a drawer in Crown Heights, about nine hundred of those drawers, a bus every twenty minutes on a weekday, and he has not used one since the second of January**; he wrote that he is not going, **and that this is the second time he has written that sentence down, and a sentence written twice is not a rule and is not a resolution and is a third thing.** Friday the eleventh: **there was no rack day**, the rack had nothing in it in it for the second time, and the man of about forty-one on no list at all came through the corridor at ten past four with a handcart, was not sent and is not going to be, and the only question asked anybody that week was *is there a form anywhere that says a rack is finished*, and the answer was nine words — **No. And there is not going to be one.** Nobody thanked him. Field Book: the pairs are twenty-nine, unmoved in fifty-eight days; the five of his own and the six of the other shape must not be merged and the sum of them is not a number.

### Chapter 162 — A Return About A Person Who Holds Water, Sent To A Firm

**Tuesday the fifteenth of September, Mireward, in a van that belongs to a firm, driven by the man of about fifty with the mower, in at about ten and back at about half past three.** *It is not a day off for you and I am not going to pretend it is.* The top track, the ground soft after Sunday's rain: **Coll Sarrowe, forty-four, on a bar, thirty-one years, the sluice belonging to a body abolished in about 1968, paid for the Tuesday out of a farm wage by the hour for ditching, and the farm does not know the Tuesday is not a ditch.** **Three men and not five**, and the order had changed: the run set facing out and not facing along, the near man taking the short throw, **and it took a man of forty-four a fortnight to work out and get right and he has not mentioned it.** The nineteenth-year-old of July, gone to his father's; the man on the near end: **I have never asked him why I take the short one and I am not going to. If you have been switched round since July then you have been switched round since July and there is nobody in this district who is going to tell you why and there is nobody in this district who wants to know.** Then a printed side out of a bag, single, headed *HOLDERS OF DRAINAGE APPARATUS — RETURN OF CONDITION*, *state the depth of water held at the time of inspection, in feet*, and at the foot *a return not received within the period stated will be completed by this office* — **it came to the farm, because a district with no address is not a place anybody writes to and a man with no address is not a person a form can be sent to, and the way this city gets hold of a man who holds water is that it writes to the firm that pays him for ditching.** The refusal, in about four seconds, correct, and it changed nothing: **a depth of water is true on a day; if I put a number in that box then in about four years a man in a room can come and ask me what the number was, and I will have to say a day, and there is no column anywhere for the day, and he will go away and write down that the return was completed by the office, and it will not be wrong, and that is the part of it that matters.** And he will not put nil in either, because **nil is a statement that somebody inspected it and found nothing, and nobody has inspected it, and if I put nil in that box then this office has got a return saying that a drain on a bank that nobody maintains has been looked at. They will not send another one. That is what I am frightened of, and it is not a fear, it is a Tuesday.** Posted back on the Friday in somebody else's envelope, and the farm did not know and nobody was told. Page home 296, token 282, plate 162, condition 215; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty weeks and six days past four months**; pairs twenty-nine, unmoved in sixty-one days.

### Chapter 163 — A Mowing Line With Three Things Stopped On It And Still One Boundary

**Friday the eighteenth of September, the flatbed in from the city at a quarter to seven, Mireward at about half past ten, four sacks on the bed, back on the Friday afternoon, in the yard at the city end at about half past four, and it does not wait either way.** Page home 299, token 285, plate 165, condition 218; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-one weeks and two days past four months.** The far half unmown since before the war; the mower man stopped four feet short of the line and shut off. **Ten feet wide, cut with a sickle, ten minutes a morning, on the way in, nine years, not on a time sheet, told to nobody until this** — told so that in about four years nobody can find out he has been over and think he did it for a reason. And: **I have not asked you why you have been out here on four Fridays since the end of June, and it is not a walk and it is not a job, and I am not going to be the one who writes down the reason.** Then the boundary, ninety seconds, and he had been waiting since the seventh of August: **there are three things at that line and it is one boundary, and the boundary is the two, and I am not one of the two, because a party is somebody who agreed and I did not agree with anybody. I am a man who has stopped. There is a difference and it took me six weeks to find it.** The handrail post four inches short, the file's *replaced like for like*, about nine families navigating by it, **and he was four feet from the only man on that bridge and did not ask him, the second time, and wrote that down.** The two sets of marks about eleven feet off, twice, and he did not go, and **he has stopped writing down what it was worth.** The cast number in the collar, in column three of a printed side in two buildings, and this time he did not read it out loud. At the third post, at about the second of four minutes: **the sound went for about four seconds, no sound at all, and under it the taste he has never put a name to and never put a place to, and it came and went in the same four seconds and was not the same as the silence and was not in it. HE DID NOT WRITE THE TIME DOWN**, which is the first time in eight months he has not done that. And on the page: **a lost sound and a bell that rings are two different things and I am not going to write them in the same column**; and **if it ever becomes a thing I will write the name of that thing in this book at the time, and until then it is four seconds and a taste and nothing, and I am not going to be the man who gives a four-second thing a name because a name is the only thing in this story that anybody can be asked for.** **Nobody has ever gone past the third post. Nobody is going to.**

### Chapter 164 — The One Good Question He Asked Anybody In Six Weeks, And What It Cost A Boy Of Seventeen

**Monday the twenty-first of September. The round went at ten past eight and came in at about four with the bag empty.** Page home 302, token 288, plate 168, condition 221; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-one weeks and five days past four months.** The thing he worked out on Sunday standing up in about nine minutes: **the office completed the row in August in about a minute and a half on a Thursday, and done is the only state a row can be in, and the only thing that stops an office completing a row is a return, and a return is a card that goes out and comes back, and there is nothing in this district that can carry anything** — and then, in about four seconds, he sat down, because **if nothing goes out then nothing comes back, and the next thing that happens to that row is that the office does to it again.** In the room with four machines, from a quarter to ten until twenty past eleven. The allocation sheet, five rows, the zero in the fen row, the line under *officer responsible* never filled in. The question, ready, and it was the only one he asked anybody in six weeks and it was a good one: **is there anything that goes out on a Friday.** No — **the Thursday bundle. It goes to the works office at about eleven and it has gone every Thursday since before you started coming in here. It is a firm. A firm is rateable. A firm has an address.** And then he asked for it in four seconds without building it: **could the card go in that bundle.** *You are asking me to put a district's returns card into a bundle going to a contractor.* Say what you are doing. — **I am going to give the eleventh street district a correspondence address, and it is going to be a firm's, and you are going to do it because nobody has ever asked you for anything in this room and I told you in August there was nothing in it anybody could want, and I was half right, and this is the other half.** And: **I am not going to be the man who stops a conversation that has been had. The card comes off on Wednesday. I will put it in the bundle at about eleven. It will be in the yard on Thursday.** Then three things, all of them before the bottom of the stairs. **One: the man of about fifty has now been given a use, and a district that a machine holds is a district that a machine can be asked for, and in about four years a room can ask that room for every card in that bundle, and the man who put them in is the man who pressed the button, and his name is not on any of them and he is the one who can find them. He did not know that when he said yes and Milo Rook did not tell him.** **Two: the eleventh street district has a correspondence address for the first time since about 1911 and it is a contractor's yard, and he has done the thing a woman of thirty-one refused to do in about two minutes on the twenty-seventh of August, by a different road, in nine seconds, and he has not told her and telling her is not the repair.** **Three: a card that goes out and comes back has to be carried, and the round is one man short.** The yard at half past four, about nine people, and **Pell Harrow, seventeen, no power of any kind, whose mark holds for a working shift and not overnight, and who has never been asked for anything by anybody in this district.** He had it ready in about eleven seconds, and **a man who has a sentence ready before he arrives somewhere has already decided that somewhere is where the sentence goes.** *The card comes on Thursday.* / *Then it comes on Thursday.* / *It has to come back. And the round is one man short.* **No.** — **I know what you are asking me. You want the round to have a thing on it. You have had no thing on it since the twenty-eighth of August and you have walked up there on your own twice, and now there is a card and you have not got a hand, and I am the hand.** And: **you have written on the facing page of your own book in your own handwriting that the round is one man short and that is not curable from inside here, and you wrote it in front of nobody and you have not taken it out. And I am not being the reason a piece of paper exists every Friday. I have got three things under a brick in this district and one of them is mine and it has been blank since the nineteenth of January and nobody has taken it and nobody is going to, and I am not putting a fourth thing on a list because a man in a good coat has run out of hands.** **The cost of the failure is not the card. The cost is that he asked the boy.** Nobody said anything in the yard; the man of forty swept on. **The seventy-eight words are still seventy-eight words and he did not say them and nobody is going to improve them.**

### Chapter 165 — Finding Aids, Which Are Not A Catalogue Item And Which Nobody Asked Her To Write

**Tuesday the twenty-second of September, the Survey School's repository beneath Crown Heights, thirty-four steps up, nine desks on the left, a plan chest, a window that does not open, a chair under it. Bettina Sallow, fifty-four, four years on the hand index at the ninth desk. She is not in eleven streets, has never been south of that bridge, and is not going to be.** The established facts, used and not re-explained: **she will not tell you a document does not exist unless she has read the whole card**, and a card in an index is one card for one named thing, the named things are rateable things with a street on them, and there is no card in it for a drain, a cover, a length, a row of shops, a fence, a woman, a market, or a hole; **the index is not a record of what happened, it is a record of what is where, and a card in an index is a claim and not the document.** The changed thing, noticed in about ninety seconds: **she had gone and looked before she wrote it down** — four pads with request columns dated, and a fifth under the blotter with the top clean and the headings *what I looked at. how far I went. what I did not open.* Since about the middle of August. **Because a request on a pad is a person who wanted something, and a request is a party, and a party is a person, and in about four years somebody can come and ask me who wanted what, and there is no column anywhere in this room for who wanted what, and I am not going to be the woman who puts a column in.** And: **if a person wants the same thing twice then I have written the same description twice and there are two pieces of paper about a place and not about a person, and two pieces of paper about a place can be asked after, and one piece of paper about a person can be asked for. And that is not yours. That is a method, and it is four years old, and the change is five weeks old.** The recovery, nine sheets, and she said it as though she were saying how many chairs there were: **a finding aid is a description of what is in a room; an index is a description of what is where and it is a claim; a finding aid is not a claim, and it is not a catalogue item, and nobody asks the office for a finding aid because there is no shelf number on one and no one has ever been able to look one up. Then nobody will ever find it. No. That is the point of it and I did not invent the point.** On the fifth sheet, fourth line: *Bundle, paper, tied with tape. Not on index. Not opened. Came in with a consignment of drainage material about 1911 from a body that no longer exists and was not asked for by anybody. Wrapper has a watercourse on the outside of it in a hand that is not a hand in this room. **Not a survey. Not a schedule. Not an index card. Nothing in it is a rateable thing.*** The refusal, correct, changed nothing: **Tell me the shelf.** No. **A number on a shelf in a room I am responsible for is a reference. A reference is a thing a person can be asked for in about four years, and if I say it then in about four years a man can say it, and then he can ask me whose hand wrote it down, and then there is a person.** He did not write it down and did not ask a second time. And then, unasked, in about eleven words, the seed of the twenty-ninth: **and there is no card in it for a funeral either. I have checked. Twice. In about four years, and the second time was about two years ago and I did it because I could not think of an example and that is not a thing anybody does in a repository. It is not a card that is missing. It is that a funeral is not a rateable thing, and it is not in a street, and it does not have a plan, and there is nobody to hold a card about it, and a person dies and there is a paper for the person and the paper is in a town eleven miles away, and in this room, in a building two hundred years old, with a street list a hundred and forty years old, there is nothing at all.** **That is not an oversight. No. That is the design. That is what an index is.** Not thanked. Page home 303, token 289, plate 169, condition 222; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-one weeks and six days past four months**; pairs twenty-nine, unmoved in sixty-eight days.

### Chapter 166 — A Return About Who Was Responsible For Keeping Something In Repair, Asked About A District

**Completed Thursday the seventeenth of September, which was a Thursday, and they complete them on the Thursdays, and it has been a Thursday thing for about eleven years; back in a building in Crown Heights on Wednesday the twenty-third, on a table in a room with about nine people in it by about half past ten.** Page home 304, token 290, plate 170, condition 223; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-two weeks exactly past four months.** The second printed side: > **MAINTENANCE RESPONSIBILITY — SEASON 3 RETURN** / *Districts in which surface or drainage works have been discontinued during the season. To be completed by the office. State the name of the person responsible for maintenance up to the date of discontinuance, and the office at which returns for the district are held.* / *A return not received within the period stated will be completed by this office.* — and in the box, in a typeface, in about a minute and a half: **R. PRYCE**, and underneath it **the office at which returns for the district are held.** **Cass Ardery, about forty-four, second-in-command on the investigation of a thing he classified, not the reporting officer and not going to be**, read it in about nine seconds: **Nobody has done anything wrong. They could not ask the district and they have not tried to and they are not going to, and the record the office holds is a contract, and a contract names the supervisor, and there is no second document in this city that says who was responsible for that road on a Tuesday in June. That is the whole of the mechanism and it took about a minute and a half and it will happen again next season for a different district.** And: **he did not sign the first one and he will not sign this one and he is not going to be asked to** — and nobody in the room thought he should be told, **because a man of fifty-four with thirty-one years in charge of a gang is not frightened of a form, and he has been given two of them in five weeks by an office that cannot write to a place, and he does not know it, and nobody is going to tell him.** Then the sentence of the book, in a department's mouth, in nine seconds: **THERE IS NO FORM IN THIS CITY IN WHICH A DISTRICT CAN SAY THAT SOMETHING HAPPENED TO IT. Not this one. Not the schedule. Not a return, not a card, not an inspection, not a condition column. There is a form for a death and a form for a rate and a form for a length of road and there is no form for a loss, and where a district has a loss then the office asks it for a figure, and the district has no figure, and the office completes the row, and the row says the district had nothing to report. That is the design. It is not a complaint and it is not a fault and nobody in this room did it and nobody in this room can undo it. And a district with nothing to report is entered as stable. And a district entered as stable is a district that cannot be frightened. And a district that cannot be frightened is a district where there is nothing to insure against.** **And Milo Rook could not say one word, because a man of sixty-one, a man of forty-four and a woman of thirty-four have all said a version of it for months, and it is now in a department's mouth as a design, and he has no answer to it — the second time in eight months that the best sentence said in a room has been said by somebody else and he has had no answer to it.** Two wrong-shaped things, noticed by him and by nobody else: **a return about responsibility for apparatus asked about a district, and answered with a contractor's supervisor when the true answer is that nobody was responsible for noticing; and a description of a place in a second field, which is correct, and in about four years a man can ask the side for the office and then the office for the desk and then for the machine and then for the man** — **a description of a place is how a person gets found.** The man of about fifty does not know and is not going to be told. The close: **the card came off the machine on Wednesday at about nine, went into the Thursday bundle at about eleven, and is in a contractor's yard; nobody has told the district; there is nowhere to tell it; a boy said no on Monday; Roy Pryce has two boxes with his name in them and does not know it; a woman of thirty-one shut a register on the twenty-seventh of August and has not been told what he did on Monday morning; and a man of about fifty pressed a button on a district for the first time in nineteen years and there is no column anywhere for that.**

### Chapter 167 — The Last Thursday Of A Month, And A District That Has An Address It Did Not Ask For

**Thursday the twenty-fourth of September, the last Thursday of the month, and the return goes to a board on the first Thursday of a month, and the first Thursday of next month is the first of October. The round went at ten past eight and came in at about four with the bag empty. He did the round's business, which is going about a district that has nothing to collect, and he was not in the records room and was not going to be.** Page home 305, token 291, plate 171, condition 224; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-two weeks and one day past four months.** Her half: **a return is a grid, a cell for every week, a figure or one or the other, and no blank, because a blank is a query and a query goes down**; the district is on the fourth page from the bottom and has not moved; **every cell on that row is a dash again this month, four of them because September had four Fridays in it — the cell count is the number of Fridays in the month, which is a rule and not a reason, and October has five, which is why the batch's last image is the fifth cell — put in with a pen, the pen being the wrong instrument, and she has used a pen on that return once in about two years.** **She did not describe the line and nobody asked her to and he did not ask.** The corridor at ten past four: **you have got a district with a correspondence address.** — **a district returns card went out of a records office about a mile from here in a bundle addressed to a contractor, and it went out at about eleven this morning, and I know what went out of it because I stood in a room with four machines on Tuesday afternoon and asked one question. I found the hole on Friday the eighteenth because a card that has been going round in a run since the middle of January has not come through my counter, and the only two places it comes from are a room with four machines and a satchel that went out with the works' papers, and the works' papers stopped on the thirty-first of August.** And: **I have been sitting with it for six days and I have not put a pen to anything, and I have worked out that I am not angry, and I would like to know what to do with the not being angry, because there is no box for it either.** The three unsafe versions of a register, from the twenty-seventh of August, and then the fourth: **and you have now given it a fourth one, and it is the worst of the four, and it is not in a book, and it is a Thursday bundle going to a firm's yard. Nobody audits a contractor's yard.** And the whole of it, in her mouth: **the rest of it is that a records counter is somewhere a department can be asked a question and a contractor's yard is not, and if the card comes back from that yard then a man in that yard has looked at it, and he will hand it to the only name in the file, and that name is R. PRYCE, and Roy Pryce will carry it out to the district on his own time on a Monday, because he is a foreman of fifty-four and he has been in charge of that gang for thirty-one years, and he will know exactly what it is and he will not be frightened by it, and he will not put his name on it, and it will go back. And then nothing arrives. And then nothing arrives, and you will put another dash in a cell, and a dash is a statement about the post, and the post has stopped. And that is the first Monday since the twenty-eighth of August on which the correct thing and the useless thing have been the same thing, and I have been in this room for two years finding the places where a thing that exists can be asked about, and there are now four of them, and you made the fourth one in nine seconds on a Monday morning, and you did not ask me, and I am not going to pretend that you asking me would have stopped it.** The disagreement, fourth time, and she says his half for him: **it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing a man with a box can ask for in about four years.** — **It is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing that exists.** **And I have said the second half for you, because you have said it four times and I have said the first half four times, and neither of us has a better one, and I am not going to have it a fifth time in a corridor with a bag over your shoulder.** Then the card: **yours is the only name on it, the line is through a paragraph, the re-run is every Friday at about four since the nineteenth of July, and tomorrow is the tenth Friday; and a card with a thing struck through it is a card with a mark on it, and there is nothing in this city that can get a machine to give a hand back, and the only way a hand comes off a card is if the card is not printed, and the card is not printed because a district with no rateable unit is not printed, and you have spent eight months refusing to make a rateable unit out of anything.** And his answer, and then the true reason, said once: **you did it because you are a man who writes things down and there was a week with nothing in it and you had nowhere to put it. And I have written that down at about eleven o'clock tonight and it is the truest thing in this entry and it is not a resolution and it is not going to be improved.** Nothing else was said about it that day. **The custody register page is the tenth week in a row he has not asked about and is not allowed to, and neither of them mentioned it in one word; he has not told her about the season; she has not asked since July and has not told him she noticed he stopped counting. Neither of them was warm, neither won it, nobody was thanked.** Pairs twenty-nine, unmoved in seventy days.

### Chapter 168 — A Yard, On The Last Morning Of A Gang, And The Sixth Of A Kind Of Thing That Is Not Mine

**Monday the twenty-eighth of September, and an ordinary day.** The Row, the man of about forty sweeping with a brush and never asked for his twenty-eight undated words, the fishmonger of about forty-five with her trays out by nine, **a man of about sixty with a shed eleven minutes away who is not on the Row on a Wednesday, and it was not a Wednesday, and he does not know that a piece of paper exists and is not going to be told.** Page home 309, token 295, plate 175, condition 228; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-two weeks and five days past four months.** The barriers up, the road open, **and eleven men and a foreman on the back length at half past seven, which is how this district found out where they had been, and it is the only thing the twenty-eighth of September added to the inventory.** Pell Harrow first, in four words: **it went back on Friday** — the card was in a yard at the north end on Thursday with a foreman's name on it, the foreman read it standing up, put it in a bin, and posted it back in somebody else's envelope on the Friday; **I was not sent and I am not going to be and I did not ask him anything and he did not say anything to me and he did not know anybody had sent it. Because he looked at the envelope first and then at the card and then at the yard, and that is what a man does when a thing has come to him that he did not order.** **Nobody is going to tell Roy Pryce that a surveyor gave a man in a room with four machines a district to hold, and nobody is going to tell the man in the room either, and the two men are each going to go on not knowing about the other for as long as either of them is in this city.** Roy Pryce loaded a barrow, a shovel, two brooms, a coil of wire and a folding barrier, squared the barrier against the wall with his boot, and at about half past twelve said one thing to nobody in particular: **there is a place about two hundred yards up the length where the verge has gone and it is not on a schedule and it is not in a condition column and it is not going to be in anything, and in about four years it will be a hole.** *Do you want it written down.* **No. If it is written down then it is a fault, and a fault is a thing somebody gets a form for, and the form goes to a firm, and the firm is me. And if it is not written down then in about four years it is a hole and I am a man who was told about a hole and said nothing, and I would rather be that.** Then the giving-up, at about twenty past one, in about four seconds, standing up, with nobody watching him decide: **A YARD IS THE ONLY INSTRUMENT IN THIS DISTRICT THAT HAS NEVER ONCE TURNED INTO A DOCUMENT**, he had known that in June, and in August he had written that he was not going to be able to use it again this year, **and the sentence was right and the year was wrong, and a year was the wrong length of time to put on an instrument** — and he gave up a hundred and ten words he had had ready since the seventeenth of July. **It cost Roy Pryce, who does not know it was a decision and it will not be told to him**, because he said the whole of his argument once, correctly, in a yard, on the twenty-fourth of August, and has said he is not going to say it again, and was right to say that. **THE SIXTH OF HIS OWN, AND NOT THE SIXTH OF THE OTHER SHAPE: five of my own, four of them about a person and one about a habit, and six of the other shape, and now six and six, and the two lists are two lists, and the sum of them is not a number, and a reader in about four years is going to add six and six together and get something that does not exist, and I have written that down in the same hand as the four words and the signature and I am not going to improve it. It is not about a habit. The rule on the facing page is the habit. This one is about a man of fifty-four with a barrow and about nine people and about a sentence that was correct, and it is the only instrument in this district I have ever given anything away on, and I gave it away standing up, and it took about four seconds, and I have not written down what the four seconds felt like and I am not going to.** Pairs twenty-nine, unmoved in seventy-four days.

### Chapter 169 — A Word On The Outside Of A Wrapper, And A Column With One Hand In It

**Tuesday the twenty-ninth of September, the thirty-four steps, and the volume's midpoint, and the Rainway is named.** Page home 310, token 296, plate 176, condition 229; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-two weeks and six days past four months.** He came with a sheet of his own paper and **nothing on it, on purpose, for the first time in eight months.** She got the bundle down herself and **neither of them said the number out loud and neither asked the other.** The refusal from the twenty-second, restated: **I am going to get it down myself and I am not going to tell you the number on the shelf, and you are not going to ask me, and last week you did not ask me, and I have not stopped thinking about the fact that you did not ask me.** The wrapper: > **RAINWAY DRAINAGE BOARD — CONSIGNMENT OF SURVEY AND SHEET MATERIAL** / *received, unasked for. not evidence. not for index.* **That is a water. It has been a water in this city's land papers since about 1911 and it is the name on nine plots of about four acres each that are held on shares, and it runs about two miles above a ford that has been dry since about 1919, and I have never once in four years heard a person say that word in a conversation and I did not know it was a word anybody used until I read it. It is the name of the water. That is all it is on the outside of that bundle.** **And he did not join it to anything**, and the refusal in full: a name on a wrapper and four bells and a bell on a post at a ford and a plate and a card and a post and a collar and a handrail are not the same thing, **and the reason I know they are not the same thing is that I could put them in one column in about four seconds and I have done it about nine times in eight months and every one of the nine was a mistake. A watercourse is a watercourse. A name is a name. And the two of them are in a bundle because a bundle of paper that came from a drainage board has the board's water on the outside of it, and that is the only reason those two things are in the same room, and it is not a reason.** The outside flap, printed in small type under a rule, and **a flap is not a catalogue item and there is no number on it, and that is the whole of the difference between the flap and the shelf**: > *Schedule of instruments* / **First Atlas** — citywide; custody distributed; gaps widened, not closed / **Singular Mode** — emergency setting; one account to become the Index's version; others suspended / **Quieting** — setting not shut down; forced output thereafter treated as the record. He wrote the three words down once, in his own hand, in the printed order, and did not underline them and did not write what he thought they were. *What are they.* — **I have not got those three words in this room in four years of cards and I am not going to guess at them. And I am not going to tell you what I think they are, because a person in a good coat writes down what a person in a good coat thinks they are, and then in about four years there is a sentence in a book in a coat with three words in it that I had a feeling about, and that is the worst kind of document there is.** And: **there is no date on the wrapper and there is no date on the flap and there is no section number, and you did not ask me for one, and a fold is not a chronology and a mark transferred this week is not older than the sheet it landed on.** Inside, about forty leaves: **a printed side headed with the name of a day — not a funeral, a day set aside, kept, announced, no collection — and at the foot of it a ruled block with one column, and the column was headed with the name of the day; and one leaf with about forty lines in it and every line in the same hand, one hand, the same pen down a column for forty entries, and the entries were names, and a rule at the top and a rule at the bottom and nothing else on it at all, and a day at the top of the sheet and no year.** And then, in about four minutes, standing up, to nobody — **the first time in eight months he has named a thing out loud in a room where two people were**, and he did not do it on purpose and has written that down: **Somebody kept a day and provided a column and forty people put their names in it in one hand, and the sheet became the account, and the account wanted one account, and there was a setting for it, and the part of a mourning that could not be made to be one account did not stop. It went out of the record. And out of the record is the weather.** And: **A mourning that is displaced from the only form that could have held it is a mourning that is still happening, somewhere, in the weather. And it is not a weapon, and nobody designed it, and nobody put it there. A department had a printed side with a column for a day, and forty people put their names in it in one hand, and then the account wanted one account, and the part of it that would not go into one account went out of the record, and out of the record is where the weather is, and it is over a fen four miles from here, on a water that has a name on the outside of a wrapper.** The Registry: **it cannot acknowledge it and it is not refusing to**, and the whole of the chain, and ground (d) on the key, and **the key is correct and I have got nothing against it and I have wanted something against the key since the third of July and I am not going to look.** And: **there is nobody to be angry at, and that has been the answer since the third of July and it has got worse every week, because every week the machinery does something correct.** The last refusal, correct, changed nothing: **What is on the second flap.** Nothing is on the second flap. You have not looked. **I have not looked and I am not going to look and that is not a rule I made for you, that is what I do. There is a column on the first leaf with forty names in it in one hand and no year, and that is a sheet about a day, and a sheet about a day is not a record of what happened on the day, it is a record of who was counted. And if I turn that flap over and there is a second sheet, then there are two sheets, and a thing that is in two places is a thing that somebody can be asked about in one of them.** And: **a repository is a room where things go that a department has decided are not evidence. A bundle is not evidence of a thing. A bundle is evidence that a department sent a thing away, and I have never in four years seen a department send a thing away that it did not have a reason for, and the reason is always the same, and the reason is that the thing could not be put in the form.** And the three: **you are not going to take that out of this room. And you are not going to write down the column. And you are not going to write down a name of the water on the outside of that wrapper in anything that goes into a building.** Not thanked.

### Chapter 170 — The Morning Of The Thirtieth, And A Word That Does Not Make It Rain And Does Not Stop It Raining

**Wednesday the thirtieth of September, and the second side of the sheet is right about today, and nobody stood in the weather in eleven streets today, and nobody has stood in the weather on a Wednesday since the eighth of April, and he is not counting them, has not counted them since the first of July, is not going to start on the last day of a month, and there is no total anywhere and there is not going to be one.** Page home 311, token 297, plate 177, condition 230; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-three weeks exactly past four months.** The morning: the Row, the brush, the trays, **nothing keeps this Row and if he stopped tomorrow the Row would be the same on Thursday as it is on Wednesday except for the grass**; the barriers up and the road open; **a rack with nothing in it in it since the fourth of September, the last Friday was the eleventh, the next is the second of October, and he did not go and look on the eleventh and is not going to on the second**; the second tin at thirty-seven. Then the word, in about four minutes at a window, and the truest line in the entry: **a word in a book does not make it rain and it does not stop it raining. The water is about two miles long and it has a name because somebody in about 1911 had to write one down, and that is the whole of the word, and I have got it, and it is in this book, and it is mine, and it does nothing. I am not going to build an instrument out of a word in a coat, and there is no form for a word in a coat and there is not going to be one.** **And he did not tell anybody** — not the woman of thirty-one, not the woman of thirty-four, not the woman of thirty-seven, not the man of sixty-one, not the man of forty-four, not the boy, not the man of about fifty, not the man of about forty-one, not the foreman of fifty-four. Then the standing question in three parts, **with the first part carrying a displaced mourning and not a form and not a practice**: (a) **it is a kept day, in about 1911, that somebody printed a side about, and a column, and forty people wrote their names in it in one hand, and the sheet became the account, and the account wanted one account, and the part of the mourning that would not go into one account went out of the record, and out of the record is the weather, over a fen, on a water called Rainway** — and the answer, entire: **when a department finally does write down a mourning, the mourning does not stop, it only changes weather; and the answer to this volume's question is not that writing is bad, because writing is not bad, and the writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything; the answer is that a form that wants a person is the mistake, and the mistake has been in a building in Crown Heights since about 1911.** (b) There is no second outlook, unmoved, **and I am not going to put a figure on it**. (c) **Eight instruments that are not one**, the eighth being a name on a wrapper, and the finding unimproved. And the state: **it is worse than it was on the seventh of September, and here is the whole of it, in the order it happened** — six things, all of them done by somebody with a job, correctly, with a form — **and not one of those is the volume's problem and all of them are the same problem, and the same problem is this: every one of them was done by somebody with a job, doing the job, correctly, with a form, on a day, in a building, and there is nobody in this story to be angry at, and there has not been since the third of July, and a woman of thirty-one at a records counter found that out in about nine seconds in July and said *there is no second use for that form* and went back to her desk, and she has not improved on it since.** And the close, an inventory and not a lament and not a resolution: **about nine people who are not on a list, a brush, trays, a man of about sixty who is not on the Row on a Wednesday, a woman of sixty on a second landing who is the ninth of twenty-six names, a boy of seventeen who said no in a yard, a foreman of fifty-four on a firm's books, a rack with nothing in it in it, a tin at thirty-seven, the barriers still up, a post that does not come, and one printed side in the whole of this city that says the works are not going to be maintained. And a word that none of them has got, in a book in a coat, in a front room, and it is the only new thing in this district since the third of July, and it does not make it rain, and it does not stop it raining, and it is not going into anything, and nobody is going to be thanked for it, and he is not going to be the author of anything, and he is not cured of anything.** And the village, and the drawer in Crown Heights, and the bus, and **in about three weeks there is going to be a choice, and it is going to be between that and a building, and a thing that is going to come to a head is a column, and a column is worse than a count.** Tomorrow is Thursday the first of October and the return goes to a board. **It is not a lament and it is not a resolution and the volume is not over.**

---

### Chapter 171 — Two Boards In One Corridor, And A Thing That Is Either Maintained Or Not Maintained

**Thursday the first of October, and the first Thursday of a month, and the return goes to a board, and he has known that for about two years and has never gone.** Page home 312, token 298, plate 178, condition 231; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-three weeks and one day past four months.** The round out at ten past eight, in at four, empty bag, **one man short, and it is not a walk and nobody has called it one.** He takes a bus — **the first one since the second of January** — to a corridor in Crown Heights about forty feet long, at about ten past ten. **THERE ARE TWO BOARDS IN IT AND THEY WERE PUT UP WITHIN ABOUT TWENTY MINUTES OF EACH OTHER ON THE SAME MORNING BY TWO OFFICES THAT DO NOT SPEAK.** The left is the return; **he did not go near it, and the reason is that a cell and a line are two different things and he is not going to stand in a corridor looking for a district on a return.** The right is new, has a crease where it was folded into three, and a thumbprint of grease on the bottom corner. It is > **SCHEDULE OF DISTRICTS — SEASON 4 (PROPOSED)** / *Column 3: districts at the present rating. Column 4: districts proposed to be withdrawn from maintenance. **A district is either maintained or it is not maintained.** Printed from the district list, which is a hundred and forty years old and has never been printed from a list of offices.* / *A district with no correspondence address will be completed by this office.* Six columns and the four grounds on a key along the foot, and the key is the same key and **he has had nothing against the key since the third of July and is not going to look.** **A schedule is a thing that is either maintained or not maintained** — he had that in about eleven seconds, wrote it on the back of his hand in pencil, and rubbed it out, because a thing on the back of your hand is a thing on your hand. **THE DISTRICT THAT IS NOT A DISTRICT ON IT:** the eleven streets is not one of the names, has never been one, and is on the schedule anyway, a third of the way down, in column four, in a line that does not begin in the name column and is a shade darker: *No district number. No correspondence address. Works contract 3rd qtr (R. PRYCE), expired 31 August. Ground (d). **NOT TO BE MAINTAINED.*** **Nobody put a lie on that board, and a set of works is not a place, and a thing that is not a place cannot be asked whether it should be there.** **A SEASON'S WORK GOES OUT WITH A FORM ABOUT SOMETHING A DEPARTMENT CAN COUNT, AND THE SOMETHING IS A LENGTH** — *Return of Scheduled Works — Season 4 (Proposed). State the length of frontage, in yards, proposed for maintenance in the season* — and **THE ELEVEN STREETS IS ENTERED AT TWO THOUSAND THREE HUNDRED AND FORTY YARDS, and Cobb Lock at four hundred and ten, and four hundred and ten yards is a quarter of a mile, and a village of a hundred and forty people is not a quarter of a mile of frontage, and the office has got the figure and cannot be asked where it got it and neither can he and there is no column anywhere for the question.** The Row, the brush, the trays, the barriers still up, the rack with nothing in it in it, the second tin at thirty-seven. **The count, done on the bus in about two minutes: the twentieth of August, the seventeenth of September, the first of October — three printed sides in fourteen days, after a hundred and forty days of nothing, and there is no column in this district that wants a number for either figure.** Field Book entry, the twenty-first line standing.

### Chapter 172 — A Landing Is Not A Rateable Thing, And She Writes The Day Backwards

**Friday the second of October, the flatbed, in from the city at about a quarter to seven, in Mireward at about half past ten, four sacks on the bed, back on the afternoon, in the city yard at about half past four, and it does not wait either way, and there is no bus and there has not been one since about 1961.** Page home 313, token 299, plate 179, condition 232; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-three weeks and two days past four months.** **Garn Ollerton, thirty-seven, twenty-six years, four landings** — *You are going to stand there for two hours and I am going to be doing the second one, and then the third, and then the fourth, and if you want to be useful you can hold the painter for about ten minutes.* **He held it for fourteen minutes and understood a fifth of it, and she does not hurry the last eight feet.** She walked him in and out of the tenth crossing; **he has never thanked her and is not going to, and a person who has thanked somebody is a person who has asked.** **THE REFUSAL, AND IT IS OFF THE PAGE AND ON A FORM AND IS ORDINARY:** *Works at Landings and Stages — Confirmation of Works. State the length of frontage, in yards* — **a length of frontage for a landing** — and she had written one word on it in pencil, underlined, in the remarks box: **NO.** *A landing is not a rateable thing. That is not mine. That is a department's. A landing is not in a street and it is not in a rate roll and it has got no frontage, because frontage is a thing you measure along an edge that faces something, and a landing is a place a boat stops, and a place a boat stops is not a length and cannot be put in yards by anybody.* And when he made a mooring number up in four seconds: *A mooring number is a place. A place is a thing you can write to. And the day you can write to a landing is the day there is somebody to write to about.* And: **put four in that box and it will be wrong, and wrong is worse than a blank, because a blank gets asked about and wrong gets believed. That is not a rule I have. That is a Tuesday.** **The finding, in about eleven seconds on a bank:** a form asks a place for a figure; a place with no figure gets asked again; a form that gets asked again is a form with a person on it; **the eleven streets had no figure so an office went to a contract, a man had no figure so he wrote no and posted it back in somebody else's envelope, and a landing has no figure at all and wrote no and did not have to be asked twice — and the difference is not character, the difference is that one of them has a piece of paper and two of them do not, and the piece of paper is what makes the office go looking.** **A word he has got and did not use:** the water is about a mile and a half from where he was standing, a woman of thirty-seven who could have taken him to the fourth landing in the dark was forty feet away for an hour and ten minutes, **and he did not say it, and he did not join it to the schedule, and the reason the two of them were in the same place is that he was in the same place.** The fourth landing is done last and **he noticed and did not ask and it is the only sentence he has about it.** Field Book entry.

### Chapter 173 — Ten Feet Wide, And A Post Four Inches Short, And Nobody Has Levelled It

**Tuesday the sixth of October, and the van is the mower man's and belongs to a firm and has got a rack on the back for mowers and a company name in a colour that has gone the colour of the weather, and it has two stops and then the yard, and it is not a day off, and he has said so both times this year without being asked to.** Page home 317, token 303, plate 183, condition 236; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-three weeks and six days past four months.** The city half, nine years, a bar and not a mower, in about an hour, the mower on the rack, **the reason being a load calculation in a drawer from about 1974 with the low end of it not re-checked.** The refusal is NOT re-staged: *There are still three things at that line.* / **There are. I said that in September and I have not said it since and I am not going to say it now. I said it to you because I had been holding it since the seventh of August and I had waited for somebody to say the wrong thing about it so that I could say the right one, and I have said it, and it is done, and I am not a party to it and there is nothing else I have got.** And the new fact: **he walked out onto the deck, past the line, about nine feet onto the far half, stopped, looked at the grass, and came back.** *That is as far as I go. I said I was not going to put a machine over it. I did not say anything about walking. In four years one of these is going to want a figure for the far half and it is going to be a figure in yards, and there is going to be a man in a room who cannot get a figure, and then somebody is going to work a figure out of a piece of paper from 1974, and that figure is going to be about two hundred and forty, and I am not going to be here when it is.* **AND THE BRIDGE IS ON THE SCHEDULE, column four, ground (a), at ONE THOUSAND AND NINETY YARDS** — read out loud once on the bank, to nobody, and written down that afternoon. *One thousand and ninety yards is the length of surface and drainage works executed on a bridge and its approaches under a third-quarter contract that expired on the thirty-first of August.* The bridge is about a hundred and forty feet and eleven inches; parapet to first post about a hundred and thirty yards; third post about ninety yards past the second; the gang's path on the far side about three hundred yards. **A boundary has two ends and no middle and cannot be measured in yards, and a mowing line ten feet wide cut with a sickle by a man on his way in is not a boundary either, and the schedule does not have a column for a place where two things stopped and it has a column for a length, and a length is the only thing it can count, and it counted one.** **THE RATE, WHICH NOBODY PUT ON THE BOARD AND WHICH IS WHY THE BOARD EXISTS: a figure on a schedule is not a measurement, it is a priced measurement, and a district with no rateable unit has no rate, and it did not need one, because the rate was on the contract, and the contract has gone — so THE ELEVEN STREETS IS NOW A SUM OF MONEY AS WELL AS A ROW AND A BOX AND A NAME, and it is the first thing in eight months that has been about money.** **The van's second stop, refused in about four seconds:** *I said two stops on the sixth and I have said two stops on the sixth and every Tuesday for about two years, and one of them is a place I get my coffee and I am not going to be a second stop for this.* And then the best sentence anybody gives him in the block: *You did not ask me at the gate. That was the mistake and it was not mine.* And *Do not write that down.* / **I was not going to.** / *You were, and I could see you.* **THE HANDRAIL POST IS FOUR INCHES SHORT, the file says replaced like for like, nobody has levelled it and nobody is going to, about nine families navigate by it, and he was about four feet from the only man who cuts both halves of that bridge for the third time since July and did not ask, and he is not going to make a column of it, because a column is worse than a count and a count is a number somebody can be given.** No Field Book entry, by design.

### Chapter 174 — A Top Track, A Bell On A Nail, And A Man Who Said Not To Come Back

**Tuesday the thirteenth of October, and the mower man's van again, and the failure.** Page home 324, token 310, plate 190, condition 243; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-four weeks and six days past four months.** He tells the driver the whole of it before they go: **I am going up a track to ask a man of sixty-one a question he told me in July not to come back and ask, and I have had the sentence ready since Friday, and I know what that means.** Four plots and **a house that is not made of brick and has no number and has never had either**; a woodstove, a table, a chair, a bed, nine books, and a jar on the table with coin in it. **BARNABY PIKE, SIXTY-ONE, digging in this district for about thirty-one years, on no rate roll, no employer, no wage, no record, no holiday, and no name on anything in this city**; he worked out who was there from about the fourth stride. **A HANDBELL ON A NAIL INSIDE THE DOOR, TWICE BEFORE HE STARTS AND ONCE WHEN HE STOPS, WITH HIS LEFT HAND WHILE HE DIGS WITH HIS RIGHT, NOT STOPPING TO DO IT. That is the whole of what the bell is on this page. Milo Rook stood about four feet away for two hours and did not ask him about it and has written down that he is not going to.** The refusal, in his words: **A yardage is a different kind of thing from a date. A yardage is about now. You can say a yardage out loud to anybody and it is a true thing about this afternoon, and if a man writes it down it does not follow me about for the rest of my life. A date does follow you. A date is a thing that happened. And there is no column anywhere in this city for a thing that happened.** And the second reason, the one that is his: **There is a jar on this table with thirty-one years of wages in it in coin and there is no wage and no employer and no name of mine on anything, and there is not one of us in this district that can be produced. If I say a yardage to a man in a good coat in a front room, then in a year somebody wants a figure about a drain, and in four years somebody wants a figure about a date, and in about nine years there is a man in a room who can produce me, and he gets there by asking one man about a length of frontage in eleven streets.** And: **I would be the first. There is nobody else to ask.** **THE COST IS NOT THE LENGTH. THE COST IS THAT HE WENT.** *He was told on Friday the twenty-fourth of July, standing in a doorway on a top track, not to come back. He has not been up that track since the twenty-eighth of August. He wrote it down twice and he went on the thirteenth of October at about half past ten in a van that belongs to a firm, with a sentence he had been carrying since Friday.* **A man who says not to come back has said a thing about the future, and there is no form anywhere for the difference between a man who was asked to come back and did not come and a man who was not asked and came, and there is not going to be one. And he cannot ask Pike whether it was all right, because the only way to ask is to come again, and coming again is the thing that was asked not to be done.** **He is NOT putting it on either of the two lists. A third list is a column and a column is a column and I have just written three sentences on the same of those two subjects and that is enough.** Not thanked. Field Book entry.

### Chapter 175 — The Fourth Landing, And A Day That Is Worked From The End Of It

**Friday the sixteenth of October, the flatbed, four sacks, and he is not on the town side for a change of order.** Page home 327, token 313, plate 193, condition 246; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-five weeks and two days past four months.** **THE CHANGED METHOD IS NOT HIS AND IT IS NOT A WORD:** on the second of October she was on the third when he arrived; today she is on the second and says *I am going to do the third, and then the first, and then the fourth.* She has done four landings in the same order for twenty-six years and she is not doing them in it today, **and he works out why in about four minutes, and it is the reason for the whole entry: a day is not worked from the front; a day is worked from the back, and you find out what it is going to cost you by working out where you are going to finish.** **She is the first person Milo Rook has ever met who writes a route backwards, from where you are going to be**; she did that in the spring in about nine minutes on a boat deck, walked him in and out of the tenth crossing, and was not asked. The fourth landing, four miles up, nobody uses it, a fence and a gap that is not a gate any more. **AT ABOUT TEN PAST ONE THE SOUND WENT** — no sound at all, and under it in the same four seconds the taste he has never put a name to and has never put a place to, and it came and went in the same four seconds and was not the same as the silence and was not in it. **HE DID NOT WRITE THE TIME DOWN, and that is the second time in eight months**, and he wrote on the Friday night that he had not written it down, **and a man who writes down that he did not do a thing has still done the thing, and there is no form for that and he is not going to build one.** It acquired nothing; it did not become the boat, the weed on the post, the four landings, a woman of thirty-seven standing eight feet away, or the name of a water. **A lost sound and a bell that rings are two different things and I am not going to write them in the same column.** He did not tell her. **The temptation: he wanted to write her day down, and did not, because a ferry route is a ferry route, twenty-six years is not a record, a jar is not a wage, and a route that cannot be produced cannot be asked after.** The recovery, on the bus, in about four minutes, not his: **start from the end** — *if I finish in eleven streets then Mireward is out, and if I finish in Mireward then the round does not go, and the round is one man short and it has had nothing in it since the twenty-eighth of August and nobody would notice for about three weeks.* **And a day has an end before it has a beginning, and she did not teach me that and she does not know she did it, and she is not going to come to eleven streets, and I am not going to ask her anything about it, and nobody is going to thank her.** Field Book entry.

### Chapter 176 — A Village's Name On A Printed Side, And It Is A Name And Not A Face

**Thursday the twenty-second of October, and they complete them on the Thursdays, and it has been a Thursday thing for about eleven years and it is not a punishment and it is not a meeting.** Page home 333, token 319, plate 199, condition 252; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-six weeks and one day past four months.** A room in Crown Heights with about nine people in it, Cass Ardery at the table.
