A single side: > **SCHEDULE OF DISTRICTS — SEASON 4 (PROPOSED) — AMENDMENT 1** / *Additional entries. Column 4: districts proposed to be withdrawn from maintenance. To be completed by the office.* And in the grid: > *COBB LOCK — District 214. No correspondence address. No maintained works of record. Ground (d). Length of frontage 410 yd. **PROPOSED FOR WITHDRAWAL.*** / *Person responsible for maintenance up to the date of withdrawal: **none of record*** **Nobody lied. A district with a name in a hundred-and-forty-year-old list can be entered in a column, and a name in a list is not a post, and those two facts have been true side by side since about 1911 and nobody has ever found it inconvenient.** **AND THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE TWO ENTRIES, IN HIS OWN HEAD, IN ABOUT NINE SECONDS, COMPARED WITH THE FIRST OF OCTOBER'S BOARD:** *The eleven streets is on that schedule as a set of works with a man's name in it, and it got in because a contract exists, and a contract has a date and the date has gone, and so it has a way out of it that nobody has to do anything about. Cobb Lock is on that schedule as a place with nobody in it, and it is a district, and a district is a district for ever, and there is no contract to end and nobody in this city to whom one could be given. A thing that is withdrawn because a document ran out can come back. A thing that is withdrawn because it is a place cannot come back at all. The difference between the two entries is one box, and the box on one of them says a man's name and the box on the other one says none of record, and the one with the man's name in it is the easier one, and I did not know that on Thursday and I know it now.* Cass Ardery, in nine seconds: *the office could not send it and could not ask it and did not try to either, and there is no maintained works of record there, and ground (d) on the key is no maintained works, nothing to insure against, and the key is correct*; and *there is no person responsible, and the form says so, and none of record is a true statement, and it is the truest thing on that sheet. Compare it with the other one. That one names a foreman of fifty-four because a contract names a supervisor, and the contract has expired, and nobody asked that man anything. This one names nobody because there is nobody. In about four years one of those two entries is going to be the whole of what either place is on the record as, and I could not tell you from this table which.* **THE OFFICE OF THE FIRST SURVEYOR: a box with a rule and nothing on the rule, at the foot, in the issuing position. It requested nothing, received nothing and answered nothing, and nobody in that room asked who is behind it, and it is a box and a rule and a place on a printed side where a name goes, and it is not a person.** **AND WHAT IS NOT IN THAT ROOM:** no face, no person from that village, nothing but a place, a number, a yardage and four words in a box — *the naming of a village is not the naming of a person, and in about four years a man in a room is going to ask the room where the thing came from, and the answer is going to be a name on a board, and a place is not a party.* **Nobody in this room is frightened of anything, and a person who is not frightened is a person who has not been told, and nobody in that room is going to tell her and there is nowhere to tell her, and nobody has done anything wrong and the wrong thing is going to happen anyway and it is going to happen correctly.** Field Book entry.

### Chapter 177 — Five Cells, And The Same Fact In Two Offices

**Friday the twenty-third of October, and this is MARA QUILL'S HALF, in close third, and Milo Rook is not the narrator of it and neither of them describes the line.** She is thirty-one and has been on the return for about two years; the district she reconciles is on the fourth page from the bottom and entries are not moved. **THE CROSS-REFERENCE, WHICH IS A THING THAT HAPPENS ABOUT FOUR TIMES A YEAR:** *CROSS-REFERENCE — SCHEDULES AGAINST THE DISTRICT LIST. To be initialed by the records side.* Nine hundred and something rows, column three at the present rating, column four proposed for withdrawal, **and in column four a name she has written a dash against every month for two years.** She puts the September return flat on the desk beside it, four cells, four dashes, and keeps her finger on the first one for about eleven seconds. *A dash is a statement about the post and not about the district. And a district that returns nothing is a district with nothing to report. And a district with nothing to report is a district where there is nothing to insure against. And that is ground (d) on a key printed on the second side of a district schedule in the middle of May, and the key is correct. **Two offices. One district. One fact, seen twice, in two buildings, three days apart, and neither office has ever sent anything to the other, because there is nothing to send it with.*** She initials in about four seconds. **She does not telephone anybody, and there is no number on the sheet, and a district with no correspondence address has no number, and the reason there is no number on that sheet is the same reason the row on her return is four dashes.** He comes at about eleven with his coat on, does not sit down, and gives it in about nine seconds; she makes him say it back with nothing in it that is not on the paper. Then: *I have got the same fact. You have got it off a wall. I have got it off my own return for two years. And neither of us can do one single thing with it.* **AND THEN THE ARCHIVE:** *A district with a number is a district with a correspondence address, and a district with a correspondence address is a district whose returns are held somewhere, and a district whose returns are held somewhere is a district somebody can ask a card from, and I have four of those places on a list and you made the fifth one in nine seconds.* And the correction she makes to him: *Going and not going are not two halves of one thing. **Going is what un-does it.*** **THE RULE, APPLIED AGAINST HIM, ABOUT A VILLAGE:** *In two years you have asked a man of sixty-one on a track for a number and a boy of seventeen in a yard for a hand and a man of about fifty in a room for a use, and you have put a contractor's yard on this district as its correspondence address, and every one of those was you deciding what a place was for. And in about four weeks you are going to work out what is best for a village of a hundred and forty people, and you are not going to ask anybody in it, and then you are going to go down there and tell them.* And hers: *A woman of thirty-four has been keeping a schoolroom of nineteen children off every piece of paper in this city for nine years. She has said no to four people this year. She has said, without being asked, that she is not going to be the woman who hands a district its first piece of stationery. And you have not asked her one word about any of it, and I have not asked her one word about any of it, and there is a bus past the end of this street every twenty minutes and neither of us has got on it.* **AND HE DID NOT SAY HIS HALF.** He had it ready; it took him about four seconds to get to the place in it where he usually says it and then he did not say it. *You were going to say something.* / **Yes.** / *Then do not.* **AND THAT IS NEW AND COLDER: she noticed he did not say it and she is not going to supply it for him this time either, and that is a different thing from last month, and she has thought about why for about four minutes and has not got a word for it.** Not said in a corridor on a Friday: **he has not told her about the season, and she has not asked since July, and she noticed some months ago that the number he used to give had stopped going up, and she has not told him she noticed.** **The custody register page is untouched for the ELEVENTH consecutive block and neither of them said one word about it.** She did not describe the line. *The twenty-ninth is a Thursday, and you are not going to be in this room, and you have not been in this room on a last Thursday in about two years, and I am not going to ask you why and you are not going to tell me.* **Neither of them was warm, neither won it, nobody was thanked.**

### Chapter 178 — Two Places, One Tuesday, And Nine Words From A Man With A Handcart

**Monday the twenty-sixth of October.** Page home 337, token 323, plate 203, condition 256; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-six weeks and five days past four months.** The round out at ten past eight, in at four, empty bag, **and it is not a walk and nobody has called it one.** The Row, the brush, **nobody keeps this row and if he stopped tomorrow it would be the same on Wednesday as it is on Monday except for the grass**; the trays; **the barriers still up and the road open and both of those are correct, and on Friday the thirty-first of October there is going to be a schedule in a building in Crown Heights that says the works are not going to be maintained, and the two facts are about four hundred yards apart and they are not the same fact and nothing in this city is going to put them together**; a rack with nothing in it in it, **and he did not go and look at it on any of the Fridays since and is not going to on the thirtieth.** **THE ARITHMETIC, ELEVEN MINUTES AT THE KITCHEN TABLE, STANDING UP:** Monday and Thursday are the round; **Friday is the flatbed, four miles the other way and back, and I sleep in my own bed**; **Tuesday is the van, which belongs to a firm, which is the mower man's, with a rack for mowers and a company name gone the colour of the weather, two stops and then the yard, and it is not a day off**; Wednesday, Thursday, Saturday, Sunday — **there is no bus and there has not been one since about 1961.** **A TUESDAY IS THE ONLY DAY OF THE WEEK HE HAS.** Then the two places, written without dates, which took about four seconds and was the hardest thing on the page: *ONE. A village eleven miles the other way. About a hundred and forty people. A schoolroom. A bell on a bracket over the door that has been there since about 1911. A bus goes past the end of the street every twenty minutes on a weekday and two of them a day go as far as that village and I have been on one of them twice.* / *TWO. A building in Crown Heights, about a mile from where that schedule was drafted, and a drawer in it, and a sheet of street numbers in the drawer, and about nine hundred of those drawers in this city, and a bus past the end of the street every twenty minutes on a weekday, and I have not used one since the second of January.* **AND THE ARITHMETIC IS NOT THE ANSWER, AND THE CHAPTER SAYS SO:** *It is not that I cannot get to both. One is a day on a bus. The other is an hour there and an hour back on the same bus. I could do both on a Tuesday and be in this front room at eleven at night. **THE ANSWER IS NOT THAT I HAVE NOT TRIED HARD ENOUGH. I HAVE GOT A TUESDAY.*** **WHAT IS ACTUALLY IN THE WAY: they are opposite, and going to one of them undoes the other one.** An objection is the only thing that can stop a schedule; an objection has to be filed against something; something has to be addressable; a number comes out of a drawer on a floor of street numbers from 1911. **So going to the drawer is how the village gets protected, and the drawer does not stop being how the village gets a number, and the number is in every column of every schedule in this city for ever, and a woman of thirty-four in that schoolroom has kept a place off paper for nine years and I have not asked her one word about it.** And if he does not go, Cobb Lock cannot be objected to and the schedule stands. **And the eleventh streets is not in this: it came in off a contract, and a contract is a thing an office gives, and that is a different errand in a different building and I have got no reason to think it is the same errand.** **THE BIND AT THE CENTRE: the thing that would tell him is in the drawer. If that floor has a sheet that covers the fen country then going there saves that village. If it does not, then going there loses her, permanently, having spent the only day he has. I cannot know which without going. And going is the choice.** **THE THIRD DOOR IS SHUT:** Bettina Sallow said on the twenty-ninth of September that a second question would get the same answer, **and he did not ask her a second time, and he is not going to write to her either, because a letter would make her a correspondent of this district, and a correspondent is a party, and a party is a person, and a woman of thirty-one went through a register of correspondents standing up in nine minutes in August and entered nothing, and she was right.** **NINE WORDS AT ABOUT TEN PAST FOUR:** *Where does a district get its number.* / **No. I carry them. I have never made one.** The man of about forty-one on no list at all, four times a day in this office since the twenty-eighth of December, never once sent anywhere, **not the man of about forty-one who holds the key to the drawer in Crown Heights and the second one is in no book**; he got the handcart through the gap, added nothing, and was not thanked, **and the number of words he says is nine and the number of occasions on which he has said anything to me since the second of December is a different number, and I counted neither today and there is no total anywhere and there is not going to be one.** **He did not decide. Not on the twenty-sixth, and not on the twenty-seventh, and the sentence about not going to the drawer has now been written down four times and it is still a sentence and not a rule and not a resolution and he has not got a word for the third thing and has not had one since the fourteenth of April.** Field Book entry.

### Chapter 179 — The Ordinary Day, And A Thing He Gave Up On Purpose About A Person

**Thursday the twenty-ninth of October, and this is the last Thursday of a month, and she reconciles the return today, and he was not in that room and did not go and is not going to write down what is in the line she has never described to anybody in about two years, and a promise about not asking is the only kind of promise this book has ever held.** Page home 340, token 326, plate 206, condition 259; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-seven weeks and one day past four months.** The morning: **a lorry to a yard at the north end on a Thursday, the second motor vehicle in this district in a week, neither of them his**; he did not ask for a lift, **because asking for a lift is asking, and he has asked enough people in eight months to last him until the end of the volume.** The Row, the brush, the trays, **and a man of about sixty with a shed eleven minutes away was on the Row at about half past ten on a Thursday and said good morning, and he does not know that a piece of paper exists and is not going to be told, and nothing that has happened since the sixth of April has touched him, and there is no form anywhere for a man who is not on anything.** The barriers, the road, the man of about forty-one with a handcart at ten past four and a good afternoon and nothing else. **THE FORMS WENT OUT AND NOTHING CAME, WHICH IS WHAT A POST IS:** the return, the amendment, nine hundred and something rows with a column and a yardage in them; **and none of it came, and none of it was going to come, and the office will complete the rows in about a minute and a half on a Thursday, and every one of them will be correct.** **THEN THE THING HE GAVE UP, AT ABOUT NINE, STANDING UP, IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, WITH NOBODY WATCHING HIM DECIDE:** he had built it on the Monday, *the bus goes past the end of the street every twenty minutes on a weekday and two of them a day go as far as that village, and I will be on one at eight in the morning, and I will be there by eleven, and I will ask a woman of thirty-four one question, and the question is what does the village want.* **AND THE REAL REASON, IN HIS OWN HAND:** *If I go there on a Tuesday then I will be told what I have already decided to do. **I have been in this position since the seventh of September and I have not decided anything, and that is the position I am proud of, and it is not a position, it is a way of not having to find out what I would do if I were told.** And if I am in that schoolroom for four hours I am going to agree with her, and agreeing with her is not asking her, and then I will come away from that village with the thing I went there for, and I will not have asked her a single question, and I will have done the thing to her that the office did to eleven streets with a contract, which is decide about a place using a piece of paper about a place.* **THE COST, AND IT IS NOT CURABLE, AND IT IS ABOUT A PERSON:** a question asked before a decision is a question and a question asked after a decision is a request to be told that the decision was right, and everybody in that schoolroom would hear the second one — **seventeen of them on a Friday, nineteen on a Tuesday, twenty-one on a Monday, a roll in a woman's head and not a sheet, and there is a gap on Mondays of two children and she has never once written down why and nobody has ever rung up for it** — **and by the middle of November there will be a printed side somewhere with a number on it, or a bus will have gone past the end of that street and stopped, and then it will not be a question. There is no instrument in this city for converting a decision back into a question and I have not gone looking for one and I am not going to build one. The cost is hers and it is mine and neither of us has been asked whether we can carry it and nobody is going to ask us.** The other two things that happened on an ordinary Thursday and neither is about a village: **Roy Pryce, a name in three boxes on two printed sides and a remark in a darker typeface on a board, who signed none of them and has not been asked about any of it, and the seventy-eight words were not said again and there is no reason on earth why he would and they are not any other number**; and the custody register page, the eleventh block, not mentioned, and the season still not told. Field Book entry.

### Chapter 180 — A Schedule Is Not A Work, And Monday The Second Of November Is A Monday

**Friday the thirtieth of October, in eleven streets all day. The flatbed came in at about a quarter to seven with four sacks and went back on the afternoon and was in the yard at the city end at about half past four, and he was not on it, and being in a place is not a day off and a person without an address cannot afford one.** Page home 341, token 327, plate 207, condition 260; **a foreman of fifty-four twenty-seven weeks and two days past four months.** **THE FOUR PRINTED SIDES, COUNTED AND DATED, AND THE COUNT IS A CHANGE:** one, the twentieth of August; two, the seventeenth of September; three, the first of October; four, the twenty-second of October. **The eleven streets has been on a printed side four times in seventy-one days, twice on a wall or a board and twice in a building, and it has been on a printed side as a thing that is not going to be maintained four times, and the first time since the fourteenth of April was the seventeenth of September, and it has never once been on a printed side as a place.** And the money: **a schedule wants a quantity, a quantity is a length, a length has to be priced, and a district with no rateable unit has no rate and did not need one, because the rate was on the contract, and the contract has gone. The eleventh streets is now a sum of money as well as a row and a box and a name, and it is the first thing in eight months that has been about money, and it took a hundred and forty years of street lists to get here and nobody chose it.** **AND THIS MORNING THE SCHEDULE TOOK EFFECT AND WHAT HAPPENED WAS NOTHING.** *There is no works contract in eleven streets, so there is no gang, no sheet, no rack, no tin going anywhere and no satchel, and there is no apparatus on that road that anybody maintains, and there has not been one since the spring. **A district with nothing to maintain cannot be withdrawn from maintaining it.** The office entered it off a contract that expired on the thirty-first of August, the contract has gone, the thing the contract was maintaining is not there any more, and so the withdrawal has nothing to act on and it is correct and it is not curable and it is not a fault.* **And it is not the same as being maintained. A schedule has three states in practice and it is a printed instrument with two of them on it, and the third is a road that is not maintained and was not maintained, and the difference between that road and a maintained road is a line in a column in a building in Crown Heights and nothing whatever on the ground, and that difference is now the whole of what this district is, and it is a line, and I cannot produce a line.** *A document that says a thing is going to happen is not the thing happening, and this city has been printing them for a hundred and forty years, and a dash is not a zero, and an absence is not a form, and a withdrawal of nothing is not a maintenance.* **MONDAY THE SECOND OF NOVEMBER IS A MONDAY:** the gang was on Mondays and Thursdays at half past seven, the sheet covered Monday to Sunday, the last walk of the season was Monday the thirty-first of August and was the forty-third since the first of April and was on no sheet, the last rack day was Friday the twenty-eighth, the second tin is at thirty-seven and there is no thirty-eight — **and the round will go out at ten past eight with an empty bag, and there will be nobody at half past seven on the back length, and the rack is not a rack day, and the barriers will still be out and the road will still be open, and both of those are correct.** The standing question in three parts, **with (a) carrying the displaced mourning and not a form and not a practice** and now carrying October's use of ground (d) **on a village of a hundred and forty people, and the key is correct, and I have got nothing against it, and there is nobody to be angry at and it has got worse every week since because every week the machinery does something correct**; **(b) unmoved, no second outlook, and he is not going to put a figure on it**; **(c) half an answer, eight instruments, the eighth being a word, and what he has added in nineteen days is not a ninth instrument but four sentences about two figures** — and **the same fact seen by a woman of thirty-one at a records counter a mile from where the schedule was drafted, who put five dashes in five cells with a pen and initialled a cross-reference in four seconds and could do one single thing about neither.** The state, at length, and then the inventory: **about nine people who are not on a list, a brush, trays, a shed eleven minutes away, a woman of sixty on a second landing, a boy of seventeen, a foreman of fifty-four with three boxes, a man of about fifty with a use and a description of a place, a man of about forty-one with a handcart, a woman of fifty-four with a bundle and nine finding aids, a man of sixty-one with a jar and no name on anything, a man of forty-four on a bank, a mower man and a sickle and nine years, a woman of thirty-seven with four landings, a man of fifty-eight with a rod and a chain who did not go out this month, a ditch keeper's envelope, a rail post four inches short, a weed line on a post, a rack, a tin at thirty-seven, a road with the barriers up, a post that does not come, a handbell on a nail, and a plate in the grass, two hundred and seven days, told to nobody.** **AND FOUR PRINTED SIDES, a schedule in force on a district that had nothing to withdraw, a village with a number and a yardage and nobody in the box where a person goes, a woman of thirty-four who is not going to be asked by anybody and was not asked this month, a word in a book in a coat, a bus past the end of the street every twenty minutes, and a man who has not decided and has got a Tuesday.** **It is not a lament and it is not a resolution and the volume is not over and Batch 0006 pays the choice.** Field Book entry, the standing last line.
